**Seeing Kṛṣṇa in the distance, Paurṇamāsī is describing him.**

***vṛndā:*** *lalite, satyam avidūra-vartī madhu-vairī \| tathā hi—*  
*sakhi kundalīkṛta-śikhaṇḍa-maṇḍalo*  
*naṭatīha tāṇḍavika-hūtir aṇḍajaḥ \|*  
*na kadāpi kṛṣṇa-mudirekṣaṇaṁ vinā*  
*madirekṣaṇe kṣaṇam api śvasity asau \|\|34\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O Lalitā, it is true. Kṛṣṇa is near. O friend, the peacock names Tāṇḍavika dances with his tail feathers forming a circle. O Lalitā with restless eyes, without seeing the black cloud of Kṛṣṇa, that peacock cannot live for a moment.**

***lalitā:*** *sakhi, dakkhiṇeṇa puṇṇāa-saṇḍaṁ pekkha ṇaṁ \|*

**Перевод:**

**O friend, look at the grove of** ***punnāga*** **trees on the right.**

***vṛndā*** *(vilokya sa-harṣam):*  
*cakraṁ vaśīkṛtavataḥ kila naicikīnāṁ*  
*vaṁśī-nināda-madhunā madhusūdanasya \|*  
*ābhīra-śekhara-gatiṁ pratipādayantī*  
*śobhā babhūva paramā paramasya yaṣṭiḥ \|\|35\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Looking in joy) Announcing his position as head of the cowherds, the staff of the most excellent boy who controls the herds of cows by the sweet sound of his flute has become most attractive.**

***lalitā:*** *na buttam dāṇiṁ pi doṇṇaṁ aṇṇoṇṇa-daṁsaṇaṁ \| kealaṁ raṅgiṇiaṁ pekkhia laaṅga-kuḍaṅgaṁ lahedi kahṇo \|*

**Перевод:**

**The two have not seen each other yet. Only Kṛṣṇa, seeing the doe Raṅginī, has gone to the clove bower.**

***vṛndā:*** *paśya paśya—*  
*visṛmarān parite hari-mūrtitaḥ*  
*parimalān upalabhya kalāvatī \|*  
*iyam itaḥ sakhi puṇḍraka-maṇḍape*  
*smita-mukhī tanu-vallīm apāvṛnot \|\|36\|\|*

*(punar nirūpya sa-kautukam)*  
*vyaktiṁ gatābhir abhito bhuvi paṁśulāyāṁ*  
*sadyaḥ padāṅka-tatibhiḥ kathitādhvano’yam \|*  
*paścād upetya nayane kila rādhikāyāḥ*  
*kampreṇa pāṇi-yugalena harir dadhāra \|\|37\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Look, look, friend! Smelling the perfume spreading everywhere from the form of Kṛṣṇa, Rādhā, smiling, hides her slender body here in the** ***mādhavī*** **bower. (Looking again with joy) Since her path indicated by her many footprints clearly imprinted everywhere in the dust on the ground, Kṛṣṇa quickly approaches from behind and covers her eyes with his two trembling hands.**

***lalitā:*** *hanta hanta esā pula{i}daṅgī vāmā līlā-kamaleṇa tāḍedi kamalekkhaṇaṁ \|*

**Перевод:**

**Ah! With hairs standing on end, beautiful Rādhā hits him with a play lotus.**

***vṛndā:*** *paśya paśya—*  
*bhrū-bhedaḥ smita-saṁvṛto na hi na hīty uktir madenākulā*  
*viśrāntoddhati pāṇi-rodha-racanaṁ śuṣkaṁ tathā krandanam \|*  
*sṛṣṭo yaḥ sakhi rādhayā muhur ayaṁ saṅgopanopakramo*  
*bhāvas tena hṛdi sthito mura-bhidi vyaktaḥ samantād abhūt \|\|38\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O friend she tries to hide her emotions from Kṛṣṇa, but those emotions reveal themselves. Her crooked brow shows disproval, her slight smile shows approval. She says, “No, no” but her voice broken with overbearing desire shows her consent. She defends herself with her hands, but the weakness of her protest because of joy shows her desire. Her crying shows her distress, but its dryness shows her bliss.**

***lalitā*** *(saṁskṛtena):*  
*prātikūlyam iva yad vivṛṇoti*  
*rādhikā rada-nakhārpaṇoddhūrā \|*  
*keli-karmaṇi gatā pragalbhatāṁ*  
*tena tuṣṭim atulāṁ harir yayau \|\|39\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Showing boldness in love with her nails and teeth, she conveys her displeasure, but Kṛṣṇa is incomparably pleased with this.**

***vṛndā*** *(vihasya):*  
*nairañjanyam upeyatuḥ parigalonmodāśruṇī locane*  
*svedoddhūta-vilepanaṁ kila kuca-dvandvaṁ jahau rāgitām \|*  
*yogautsukyam agād ūraḥ sphurad iti prekṣyodayaṁ saṅgināṁ*  
*rādhe nīvir iyaṁ tava ślatha-guṇā śaṅke mumukṣāṁ dadhe \|\|40\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Laughing) O Rādhā! Your eyes full of tearsof joy have washed away your mascara.Your breasts covered withperspiration have given up their red** ***kuṁkuma.*** **The trembling of your chest shows your desire for union with Kṛṣṇa. Your undergarmenthas also become loosened. Seeing all this, I think the garment desires to undo the knot.**

***lalitā:*** *kadhaṁ edaṁ biaḍḍha-miunaṁ māhavī-kuṇḍaṅgantaridaṁ saṁbuttaṁ \|*

**Перевод:**

**Why has the two skilful at love hidden themselves in the** ***mādhavī*** **bower?**

***vṛndā:***  
*rādhā-mādhavayor medhyāṁ*  
*keli-mādhvīka-mādhurīm \|*  
*dhayan nayana-bhrṅgeṇa*  
*kas tṛptim adhigacchati \|\|41\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Drinking the pure offering of Rādhā and Mādhava, sweet with intoxicating pastimes, who could be completely satisfied by drinking it with the bees of their eyes?**

***lalitā:*** *halā ede galanta-marandaṁ pi māhavi-puppha-sandohaṁ mukkia kīsaṁ bhiṅgā pubbāhimuham dhāanti \|*

**Перевод:**

**O friend, why have the bees given up the** ***mādhavī*** **flowers, though they flow with honey, and instead rush to the east?**

***vṛndā:*** *sakhi, vimucya mādhavī-maṇḍapaṁ nāgara-maṇḍalottaṁsau prasthitau tayor āmodam anusarpantaḥ ṣaṭpadā dhāvanti \| tad ehi latā-mandirālokanena nandayāvaś cakṣusī \| (iti parikramya) lalite, paśya paśya—*

*manohārī hāra-skhalita-maṇibhis tāra-taralaiḥ*  
*parimlāyan-mālyo milita-puraṭālaṅkṛti-kaṇaḥ \|*  
*ayaṁ kuñjas talpīkṛta-kusuma-puñja-praṇayavān*  
*samantād uttuṅgaṁ piśunayati raṅgaṁ murabhidaḥ \|\|42\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O friend, the bees follow the fragrance of the couple, best of lovers, who have departed from the** ***mādhavī*** **bower. Look, look, Lalitā! This bower everywhere indicates the intense pastimes of Kṛṣṇa, attractive with the scattered pearls and center jewel from the necklace, with wilted garlands, fragments of golden ornaments and piles of flowers used as a bed.**

***lalitā*** *(nipuṇaṁ nirūpya saṁskṛtena):*  
*kṛṣṇāṅga-saṅgama-milad-ghusṛṇāṅga-rāgā*  
*rādhā-pada-skhalad-alaktaka-rakta-pārśvā \|*  
*sindūra-bindu-cita-gharma-jalokṣiteyaṁ*  
*dhūnā dhinoti nayane mama puṣpa-śayyā \|\|43\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Looking closely) The scattered flower bed, colored with** ***kuṁkuma*** **which touched Kṛṣṇa’s limbs, its sides red from the lac fallen from Rādhā’s feet, is sprinkled with perspiration tinted with traces of vermilion.**

***vṛndā*** *(sa-vismayam):*  
*cikrīḍa yā rajasi rañjita-sūtra-naddha-*  
*gokarṇa-mātra-cikurā nava-biddha-karṇī \|*  
*seyaṁ kutaḥ pravara-vibhrama-kauśalāni*  
*rādhādhyagīṣṭa bata vairajitaṁ jigāya \|\|44\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Surprised) With newly pierced ears and locks of hair only as long as a cow’s ear and tied with colored thread, she used to play in the dust. From which guru has she learned such great skill in coquetry? She has conquered the conqueror of enemies.**

***lalitā*** *(pūrvataḥ prekṣya): buṁde, pekkha ṇādidūre sa-rāhā māhabo \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Looking east) O Vṛndā, Look. Kṛṣṇa is with Rādhā not too far away.**

***vṛndā:*** *śṛṇuvaḥ, kim āha saṁskṛtena rādhā?*

**Перевод:**

**Let us listen. What is Rādhā saying in Sanskrit?**

*(****nepathye****):*  
*kuru kuvalayaṁ karṇotsaṅge lavaṅgam abhaṅguraṁ*  
*vikira cikurasyāntar-mallī-srajaṁ kṣipa vakṣasi \|*  
*anagha-jaghane kādambīṁ me pralambaya mekhalāṁ*  
*kalayati na mām ālī-vṛndam hare niralaṅkṛtam \|\|45\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Offstage) Make me lotus ornaments for my ears. Sprinkle firm clove flowers in my hair. Throw a jasmine garland on my chest. Hang a belt of** ***kadamba*** **flowers on my pure hips. O Kṛṣṇa, my friends should not see me without ornaments.**

***vṛndā*** *(smitaṁ kṛtvā):*  
*vahantī mañjiṣṭhāruṇita-tanu-sūtrojjvala-rucīn*  
*nakhāṅkān khelormi-skhalita-śikhi-pakṣāvalir iyam \|*  
*sphuran-muktā-tulyair alaghu-ghana-gharmāmbubhir alaṁ*  
*samṛddhā me medhāṁ madhumathana-mūrtir madayati \|\|46\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Smiling) With nail marks glowing brightly like thin threads dyed with** ***mañjistha*****, with peacock feathers displaced by the waves of pastimes, covered with profuse drops of perspiration like sparkling pearls, the form of Kṛṣṇa, who agitates even Cupid, intoxicates my mind.**

*(tataḥ praviśati kṛṣṇaḥ prasādhitāṅgī rādhā ca \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(Kṛṣṇa enters with Rādhā, who is decorated.)**

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  
*nītaṁ te punar-uktatāṁ bhramarakaiḥ kastūrikā-patrakaṁ*  
*netrābhyāṁ viphalīkṛtaṁ kuvalaya-dvandvaṁ ca karṇāpitam \|*  
*hāraś ca smita-kānti-bhaṅgibhir alaṁ piṣṭānupeṣīkṛtaḥ*  
*kiṁ rādhe tava maṇḍanena nitarām aṅgair asi dyotitā \|\|47\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Your curling locks of hair make your** ***tilaka*** **decorations drawn with musk useless. Your eyes are so beautiful that they make the two lotus flowers on your ears useless. The waves of the beauty of your smile have pulverized your necklace. O Rādhā! What is the use of you wearing all these decorations? You are already made splendid by the beauty of your limbs.**

***ubhe*** *(upasṛtya): sundara idaṁ parama-mañjulaṁ vasanti-kusuma-maṇḍanam \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Approaching) O beautiful Kṛṣṇa, the decorations using spring flowers are most attractive.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(stavakita-dvandvam ādāya sa-harṣam):*  
*dhyeyena mukta-vṛndasya*  
*kāmyamānā muhur mayā \|*  
*yuktā tvam atimuktānāṁ*  
*śreṇyā suśroṇi sevitum \|\|48\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Taking two flower clusters in joy) It is proper that you are served by the** ***atimukta*** **flowers since I, the object of meditation for liberated souls, desire you.**

*(iti rādhām avataṁsayati \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(He puts the flowers in Rādhā’s hair.)**

***nepathye:***  
*anuparamati yāme kāmam ahnas tṛtīye*  
*jalada-samaya-lakṣmīr yauvanojjṛmbhaṇe’dya \|*  
*nava-yavasa-kadambais tarpitānāṁ kadambaḥ*  
*kalayati surabhīnāṁ gokulāyābhimukhyam \|\|49\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Offstage) Though the third** ***yāma*** **of the day is not over, today, with the manifestation of the monsoon’s beauty everywhere, the groups of cows, satisfied with the new grass, head towards Gokula.**

***lalitā:*** *rāhe anujāṇehi \| ratti-maṇḍana-ttham dullahaṁ basanta-kusumaṁ gehṇissaṁ \| (iti niṣkrāntā \|)*

**Перевод:**

**O Rādhā, please give permission for me to leave. I will gather rare spring flowers for decorating you tonight. (Exits)**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(smitvā janāntikam): vṛnde kiñcid vinodaṁ vidhātu-kāmo’smi \| tad atra priyāyāḥ pratyāyiteyaṁ puro drumādhirūḍhā kakkhaṭī tvayā mat-pakṣa-grāhiṇī kriyatām \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Smiling, aside) O Vṛndā, I wish to perform some sport. The confidential agent of Rādhā, Kakkhaṭī, is in the tree in front. You should make her act on my behalf.**

***vṛndā:*** *bhavatu, yatiṣye \|*

**Перевод:**

**Yes, I will try.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(rādhām avetya): priye candrā... (ity ardhokte kṛtrima-sambhramaṁ nāṭayati \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(Approaching Rādhā) Dear, candra… (He shows false confusion.)**

***rādhikā*** *(sa-khedam): haddhī haddhī \| kadhaṁ ebbaṁ suṇantaṁ bi ṇa me phuḍidaṁ kaṇṇa-jualaṁ?*

**Перевод:**

**(Lamenting) What great misfortune! Why do my ears, though hearing that sound, not burst?**

***vṛndā*** *(sva-gatam):* *picchikā-bhramaṇena kakkhaṭikam unmādya harer abhīṣṭaṁ vyāhārayiṣye \| (ity alakṣitaṁ tathā kṛtvā prakāśam) sakhi, raṅge mā bhaja vaimukhyam \|*

**Перевод:**

**(To herself) By waving this peacock feather, I will enchant the monkey Kakkhaṭikā and make her say what Kṛṣṇa wants. (She does this without others seeing, and then reveals herself.) O my friend Rādhā, do not be averse to joy.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *priye candrānane, kim ity akāṇḍe vimanaskāsi?*

**Перевод:**

**O beloved with moon-like face, why are you depressed without reason?**

*(****nepathye****) sāmiṇi imiṇā tujjha muddhattaṇeṇa lalidā na jībissadi \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Offstage) O mistress, because of your foolishness, Lalitā will not live.**

***rādhikā*** *(ūrdhvam ālokya sva-gatam): ṇīsaṁdehahmi kidā kakkhaḍiāe \| (prakāśam) paaṇḍaṁ kkhu kulisa-bipphūjjidaṁ kadham ḍiṇḍimāḍambareṇa saṁvaraṇijjam hodu \| (iti parāṅ-mukhī bhavati \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(Looking upwards, to herself) Kakkhaṭikā has dispelled my doubts. How can the fierce roaring of thunder cover the rattle of a drum? (She turns away)**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(apavārya):*  
*samaroddhura-kāma-kārmuka-śrī-*  
*vijayi-bhrū-yugam ākulākṣi-padmām \|*  
*vidhurī-kṛtam apy ati-krudhāgre*  
*mama rādhā-vadanaṁ mano dhinoti \|\|50\|\|*

*(iti rādhā-paṭāñcalam uccālya) sundari! madhureṇa samāpyatāṁ madhu-vihāra-kautukam \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Aside) With brows conquering the beauty of the bow of Cupid, fierce at fighting, with confused lotus eyes, distressed by extreme anger, Rādhā’s face attracts my mind.**

**(He raises Rādhā’s cloth.) O beautiful Rādhā! The joy of spring pastimes should conclude sweetly.**

*(punar* ***nepathye****) haddhī haddhī! bho pa{u}mā-sikkhe duṭṭha-sārasi tumaṁ pi maṁ kaḍakkhasi \| tā kīsa parāṇaṁ dhāremi?*

**Перевод:**

**(Offstage) What great misfortune! O student of Padmā, evil** ***sārasi*** **bird! Even you stare at me. How can I maintain my life?**

***rādhikā*** *(niśamya sa-roṣam apasarpantī): buṁde! paraṁ kettiaṁ viḍambidahmi \| tā jhatti bārehi naṁ kabaḍa-paripāḍī-ṇāṭaa-sūttadhāraṁ bhuaṇa-mārārambhi-muralī-sikkhā-ṇīsaṅkaṁ karāliā-nattiṇī-kīlā-kuraṅgam \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Hearing, she withdraws in anger) O Vṛndā! How much longer can I be ridiculed? Stop the cheating director who fearlessly teaches the flute to kill the world and is the puppet of Candrāvalī.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(sānanda-smitam): sakhi vṛnde! prasādaya rādhām \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Smiling blissfully) O my friend Vṛndā! Conciliate Rādhā.**

***vṛndā:*** *priya-sakhi rādhe! vidagdha-vadhūnāṁ mūrdhany asi \| tad akāṇḍe kaṭhora-māna-kāṇḍena nāpasāraya vallabha-kṛṣṇasāram \|*

**Перевод:**

**My dear friend Rādhā! You are the leader of all skilful women. Do not chase away the affectionate deer with the stick of your cruel mind without reason.**

***rādhikā*** *(bāḍham avajñām abhinīya): ettha avatthāduṁ ṇa juttahmi \| (iti niṣkrānta):*

**Перевод:**

**(Feigning contempt) It is not proper for me to stay. (She exits.)**

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *vṛnde, balīyasi roṣānale sāma-mādhvīkam uddīpanāyaiva \| tad alam atrānuyātrayā*

**Перевод:**

**O Vṛndā! When she has fiery anger, the liquor of pacification will only incite her. What is the use of following her?**

***vṛndā:*** *kim atra yuktam?*

**Перевод:**

**What is to be done here?**

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *vṛnde, vara-varṇinī-veśena rādhāṁ prasādhayitum icchāmi \| tad atra bhavatyā samādhānam adhyavasīyatām \|*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā, I desire to subdue Rādhā by wearing the dress of a most beautiful girl. You should try to arrange this.**

*(vṛndā saṅgīkāraṁ smitaṁ karoti \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(Agreeing, she smiles.)**

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *sakhi, gaurāṅga-rāga-saṅgataṁ varāṅganā-veśa-sādhanaṁ katham atrābhilipsye?*

**Перевод:**

**Friend, where can I obtain the dress of a girl and assume a golden complexion?**

*(praviśya)* ***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *pia-baassa, atthi gaurī-ghare tahāviha-besa-samaggī jā pa{u}māe maha hatthe samappidā \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Etering) Dear friend, all the ingredients for dressing are there in Durgā’s temple. They have been given to me by Padmā.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(sa-harṣam): vṛnde, gaurī-gṛha-gambhīrikāyāṁ bhaviṣyāmi \| tad ātma-bhaginī-bhāvena sambhāvanīyo’ham \| (iti sa-vayasyo niṣkrāntaḥ \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(Joyfully) O Vṛndā, I will remain deep within the Durgā temple. I will disguise myself as your sister. (He exits with Madhumaṅgala.)**

*vṛndā (parikramya dūre dṛṣṭiṁ kṣipantī):*  
*campaka-lavaṅga-bakulāny*  
*avacinvantyor vayasyayor atra \|*  
*sphuṭam idam eva sa-lajjaṁ*  
*rādhā vṛttaṁ nivedayati \|\|51\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Walking, glances in the distance) While her two friends pick** ***campaka,*** **clove and** ***bakula*** **flowers, Rādhā tells them clearly what happened with shame.**

*(praviśya tatha-vidhā) rādhā: sahi, tado haṁ aṇuṇeduṁ pa{u}ttaṁ ṇaṁ abahīria ettha pattahmi \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Entering, while speaking to them) O friend, disrespecting him, though he wanted to pacify me, I came here.**

***lalitā:*** *rāhe, na kkhu tumahmi kahṇassa gotta-kkhalidaṁ sibiṇe bi sambhāvīadi \| tā pa{i}di-pamattāṇaṁ pasūṇaṁ palābe kida-bīsambhā tumaṁ bañcidāsi \|*

**Перевод:**

**O Rādhā, Kṛṣṇa cannot become confused about your name even in his dreams. You have been cheated by having faith in the talk of animals which are by nature dumb.**

***viśākhā:*** *haddhī haddhī! lalide, pekkha—ajja sohagga-puṇṇimāhe āraddha-saṅgharisā baliṇo paḍipakkhā \| tā viḍambidā hma debeṇa \|*

**Перевод:**

**Misfortune, misfortune! O Lalitā, look! Today, on the auspicious full moon, our strong rivals have become jealous. We have been deceived by fate.**

***lalitā:*** *bisāhe saccaṁ kahesi \| ettha mahusave ja{i} ahmāṇaṁ muha-māliṇṇaṁ sabbattīo pekkhissanti tado solluṇṭhaṁ kaḍukkhantīo hasissanti \|*

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā, you speak the truth. If the competitors see our depressed faces during the great festival, they will stare at us sarcastically and laugh like monkeys.**

***rādhikā*** *(sva-gatam): sāhu sahīo mantenti \| tā kiṁ ettha saraṇaṁ?*

**Перевод:**

**(To herself) My friends give good advice. So what should we do now?**

***vṛndā*** *(upasṛtya): lalite, rāmānujasya nideśena rāmam upanetuṁ prasthitāsmi \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Approaching) Lalitā, on Kṛṣṇa’s request, I am going to bring Balarāma.**

***lalitā:*** *kiṁ tti?*

**Перевод:**

**Why?**

***vṛndā:*** *vasanta-śrī-darśanāya \|*

**Перевод:**

**So he can see the beauty of spring (in the monsoon season).**

***viśākhā:*** *sahi buṁde, kkhaṇaṁ bilambia kuṇa saṁdhiṁ \|*

**Перевод:**

**O friend Vṛndā, wait awhile and arrange a meeting.**

***vṛndā:*** *satyaṁ jānīhi—mayā duṣkaro’dya sandhiḥ \|*

**Перевод:**

**I know in truth that I will have difficulty in arranging a meeting today.**

***viśākhā:*** *kadhaṁ bia?*

**Перевод:**

**Why?**

***vṛndā:*** *pṛcchatām ātma-sakhī, yayādya kaṭūktibhir aparañjitaḥ kañjekṣaṇaḥ \|*

**Перевод:**

**Ask your friend Rādhā. She displeased Kṛṣṇa with her sharp words.**

***rādhikā*** *(niḥsvasya): halā buṁde, tumam ccea gadī \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Sighing) My friend Vṛndā, you are my refuge.**

***vṛndā*** *(sa-vyāja-roṣam):*  
*asūyā caṇḍālī hṛdi padam itā caṇḍi viviśur*  
*na vācas te pathyāḥ śruti-saraṇi-sīmāñcalam api \|*  
*idānīm audāsyaṁ vaśaga-madirākṣi-tatir agān*  
*mukundo nirdvandvī-bhava sakhi mudhā niḥśvasisi kim \|\|52\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(With false anger) The low class woman called spite has appeared in your heart and thus beneficial words cannot even approach your ears. Under the control of other beautiful women, he has become indifferent to you. Transcend dualities! O friend, why do you sigh uselessly?**

***lalitā:*** *kahiṁ so kkhu mohaṇo?*

**Перевод:**

**Where is that attractive person?**

***vṛndā:*** *gaurī-sadmani \|*

**Перевод:**

**He is at the Durgā temple.**

***lalitā:*** *kim karedi?*

**Перевод:**

**What is he doing?**

***vṛndā:*** *nikuñja-vidyayā sārdhaṁ goṣṭhīṁ tanoti \|*

**Перевод:**

**He is meeting with Nikuñja-vidyā.**

***tisraḥ:*** *sahi, kā kkhu ṇiuñja-vijjā?*

**Перевод:**

**Who is Nikuñja-vidyā (knowledge of the bowers)?**

***vṛndā*** *(sphuṭaṁ vihasya): aho maugdhyaṁ kiśorīṇāṁ, yad amūr ati-prasiddhām api nikuñjavidyāṁ na vidanti!*

**Перевод:**

**(Laughing conspicuously) How foolish are young girls that they do not know the famous Nikuñja-vidyā!**

***tisraḥ*** *(sa-lajjam): sahi kadhehi saccaṁ \| ṇa jāṇīhma \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Ashamed) Tell us the truth. We do not know.**

***vṛndā:*** *hanta bho viśuddhāḥ! kā nāma sā gokule ballava-balikāsti yā khalu svasāraṁ me bhāṇḍīra-devatāṁ na jānīte \|*

**Перевод:**

**What cowherd girl is there in Gokula who does not know my sister, the** ***devatā*** **of Bhāṇḍira?**

***lalitā:*** *buṁde, dehi tumaṁ mantaṁ jeṇa edaṁ vesammaṁ suhodakkaṁ bhave \|*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā, please give us a mantra by which this disturbance will have a joyful conclusion.**

***vṛndā:*** *sakhi, gokulānanda-nigūḍha-viśrambha-maṇi-mañjuṣeyaṁ nikuñjavidyā \| tad enāṁ bhajema \|*

**Перевод:**

**Friend, Nikuñja-vidyā is a receptacle of the jewel of deep faith in Kṛṣṇa. Let us worship her.**

*(iti sarvāḥ parikramanti \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(Everyone goes)**

***rādhikā:*** *buṁde edam ccea gaurī-maṇḍabam; tā ettha pavisia saṇṇāe kaḍḍhe ṇiuñjavijjāṁ*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā, this is the temple of Durgā. Enter it and bring out Nikuñja-vidyā by calling her name.**

***vṛndā*** *(kṛtodgrīvikam ālokya sva-gatam): hanta gaurīm iva kiśorīṁ dvāri paśyāmi \| (prakāśam) sakhyaḥ, kevalam ekatra bhāṇḍīra-devataiva śikhaṇḍena kuṇḍalaṁ kurvatī vartate \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Bending her neck and looking, she speaks to herself.) Ah! I see a golden young girl at the door. (Aloud) Friends, the** ***devatā*** **of Bhāṇḍira is here alone, making an earring from a peacock feather.**

***tisraḥ:*** *asacca-saṁsini, ciṭṭha ciṭṭha \| jam eso tāṇḍavia-sihaṇḍī paṅgaṇe ciṭṭhadi \|*

**Перевод:**

**O liar, stop, stop. Kṛṣṇa’s peacock Tāṇḍavika is in the yard.**

***vṛndā:*** *hanta bho dākṣiṇya-śūnyāḥ! svayam āgatya samīkṣyatām \| kim atrānumānena?*

**Перевод:**

**Ah, girls you are devoid of manners! Come yourself and examine. Why speculate?**

***lalitā:*** *halā phuḍaṁ tandāuladā canda{i}ṇo jādā, jaṁ ṇikkumanto candaa-maṇḍalī imiṇā ṇa lakkhido \|*

**Перевод:**

**Friend, the peacock must have become sleepy so that it did not see Kṛṣṇa leave the temple.**

***rādhikā:*** *halā, gharaṁ pavisia ṇiuñjavijjāṁ pucchahma \|*

**Перевод:**

**Friend, entering the room, we will ask Nikuñja-vidyā.**

*(iti sarvāḥ praveśaṁ nāṭayantī \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(All enter.)**

*(praviśya)* ***jaṭilā:*** *bhaṇidahmi pemmeṇa pa{u}māe—ajje jaḍile! diṭṭhiā baḍḍhasi \| goaḍḍhaṇo bia tuhma putto bi go-koḍīsaro hubissadi \| jam diṭṭhaṁ mae ajja gaurī-titthe rāhie gaurī ārāhīadi tti \| tā gadua bahūdiham āsisāhiṁ baḍḍhayissaṁ \| (iti parikramya raṅgiṇīm aṅgane dṛṣṭva sānandam) sāhu pa{u}me sāhu \| asacca-bhāsiṇī nāsi \| (punar nibhālya sa-khedam) haddhī haddhī \| kahaṁ gaurī-siṁhassa sire taṇḍavio ciṭṭha{i} \| tā parāvaṭṭia puttaṁ āṇissaṁ\| (iti dhāvantī niṣkrāntā \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(Entering) Out of affectionate, Padmā said to me, “Respected Jaṭilā, your fortune increases. Like Govardhana, your son will have millions of cows, for I saw Rādhā worshipping Durgā at Gaurī-tīrtha.” I will go there and make her prosperous with my blessings. (Walking, she sees Rādhā’s deer in the yard and becomes happy.) “Good, Padmā, very good. You have not lied.” (She looks again.) What misfortune! Why is Tāṇḍavika standing on the head of Durgā’s lion? I will go and get my son.(She exits, running.)**

***rādhikā*** *(janāntikam): sahio, pekkhadha loottaraṁ kiṁ pi gaurīe saundariaṁ \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Aside) O friends, look at the extraordinary beauty of the golden girl.**

***sakhyau:*** *halā saccaṁ saccaṁ \| ṭhāne kahṇassa pemma-vīsahma-saṁbhāvidā esā \|*

**Перевод:**

**O friend, it is true. She is endowed with intimate prema for Kṛṣṇa.**

***rādhikā:*** *ṇaṁ adiṭṭha-pūbbaṁ saṁbhāsiduṁ sa-saṁbhamahmi \| (ity apatrapaṁ nāṭayati \|)*

**Перевод:**

**I am eager to speak with that girl whom I have never seen before. (She becomes shy.)**

*(****nepathye****) yāmi vṛnde! nūnam rādhayā nāhaṁ paricīye \| mayā tu sahasra-dheyam anubhūyamānāsti \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Offstage) I have come, Vṛndā. I have not been introduced to Rādhā though I have experienced her a thousand times.**

***vṛndā*** *(sva-gatam):* *citraṁ sākṣād aṅganā-kaṇṭha-dhvanir evāyam \|*

**Перевод:**

**(To herself) That is the voice of a woman. How remarkable!**

***rādhikā:*** *buṁde, ṇa jāṇe kīsa pasahaṁ ṇiuñjavijjāe siṇijjhadi me hiaaṁ \|*

**Перевод:**

**I do not know why my heart is so strongly attracted to Nikuñjavidyā.**

***vṛndā:*** *sakhi, tattvaṁ jāne \| na citram idaṁ yad asāv api ciraṁ tvayy anurajyati \|*

**Перевод:**

**Friend, I know the truth. It is not surprising. She has been attracted to you for a long time.**

***rādhikā*** *(sānandam anusṛtya): halā ṇiuñjavijje! kahiṁ so tuha ṇiuñja-nāaro?*

**Перевод:**

**(With bliss she follows Vṛndā.) My friend Nikuñja-vidyā! Where is your lover who resides in the bower?**

*(****nepathye****) sakhi, kas taṁ jano jānāti?*

**Перевод:**

**(Offstage) Friend, who knows him?**

***lalitā:*** *sahi ṇiuñjavijje, muṁcehi parihāsa-cchalam \| appavaggo de ahmāriso jaṇo \|*

**Перевод:**

**O my friend Nikuñja-vidyā, please give up the pretense with joking. We are of the same type as you.**

*(****nepathye****)*  
*bāḍhaṁ tattvam avijñāya*  
*tapyamānaḥ kṛśānunā \|*  
*kathaṁ śārada-padmākṣi*  
*pāradaḥ parilabhyate \|\|53\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Offstage) O girl with eyes like autumn lotuses! Without knowing the method, how can you obtain mercury heated by fire (gold)?**

***vṛndā*** *(janāntikam)*  
*smerā kapola-pālī*  
*śaṁsati dūtyaṁ nikuñjavidyāyaḥ \|*  
*rādhe mṛdulaya tad imāṁ*  
*snehenābhyajya bhavyena \|\|54\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Aside) Her smile to her ears declares that Nikuñja-vidyā will be your messenger. O Rādhā! Embracing her, soften her with your auspicious affection.**

***rādhikā:*** *halā ṇiuñjavijje! kīsa buṁdebba ṇāṇubaddhasi siṇeha-bandham?*

**Перевод:**

**O my friend Nikuñja-vidyā! Why are you not bound with affection to me like Vṛndā?**

*(****nepathye****)*  
*vidhiḥ padme pādau nava-kadalike sakthi-yugalīṁ*  
*mṛṇāle dor-dvandvaṁ tava śaśinam āpādya vadanam \|*  
*mṛdūnām arthānāṁ na kaṭhinam avaṣṭambhakam ṛte*  
*sthitiḥ syād ity atra vyadhita hṛdayaṁ nūnam aśanim \|\|55\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Offstage) The creator, making your feet into lotuses, making your thighs into young banana trunks, making your arms into lotus stems and making your face into a moon, understanding that soft objects could not be permanent without a hard support, he made your heart into a thunderbolt.**

***rādhikā:*** *buṁde, pekkha sāṇurāa-hāsaṁ parihasijjāmi ṇiuñjavijjāe \| tā gadua milissaṁ \|*  
*(iti niṣkrāntā \|)*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā, look at her attractive smile. Nikuñja-vidyā is joking with me. I will go and meet her. (She exits.)**

***vṛndā:***  
*gokula-rāmā-preyasi*  
*nikuñjavidye kaṭhora-dhīs tvam asi \|*  
*yat-pravaṇām api purataḥ*  
*parirabhya sakhīṁ na rañjayasi \|\|56\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Beloved of the gopīs, Nikuñja-vidyā! You have a hard heart since you do not embrace and conciliate her though she is respectful and just in front of you.**

***viśākhā:*** *iaṁ rāhī ṇiuñjavijjaṁ pariraddhuṁ bhua-vallīṁ ullāseṁtī pemma-bīsaddhaṁ jappadi \|*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā, extending her arms to embrace Nikuñja-vidyā, speaks with great prema.**

*(nepathye) halā bhāṇḍīra-deade! pekkha—goula-pavesa-velā paccasīadi \| tā karijja{u} tṛṇṇaṁ ahmesu līlā-raṅga-saṅgamido kahṇassa ppasāo \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Offstage) O friend,** ***devatā*** **of Bhāṇḍira, look. The time to enter Gokula has come. May the mercy of Kṛṣṇa with his pleasant pastimes quickly be granted to us!**

***lalitā:*** *buṁde, esā tujjha bahiṇī rāhiaṁ parirambhia cumbedi \|*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā, your sister embraces and kisses Rādhā.**

***viśākhā*** *(sa-śaṅkam): diṭṭhā ṇillajjiāe tujjha ṇiuñjavijjāe purisa-dhamma-luddhadā \| jaṁ esā rāhā-bakkhoruhe ṇaharaṅkuram appedi \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Worried) I notice that your Nikuñja-vidyā acts like a man without shame, for she is scratching Rādhā’s breasts with her nails.**

***vṛndā*** *(sa-smitam): sakhi mābhyasūyaṁ kṛthāḥ \| premotkarṣa-vilāso’yam \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Smiling) Friend, do not be indignant. This is a pastime of the highest love.**

*(praviśya sotkampā)* ***rādhikā*** *(sa-bhrū-bhaṅgam): buṁde juttaṁ juttam ahmesu tuhma jihmattaṇaṁ \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Entering while trembling, frowning) Vṛndā, it is quite possible that you have deceived us.**

***vṛndā*** *(vihasya): sakhi, na vedmi kiṁ tavākūtam \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Laughing) Friend, I do not understand. What is your insinuation?**

***sakhyau*** *(sa-smitam): buṁde, viṇṇādā de mohiṇī-bhūdā ṇiuñjavijjā \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Smiling) Vṛndā, we have understood that Nikuñja-vidyā has arisen by your illusory power.**

*(tataḥ praviśati sa-putrā)* ***jaṭilā:*** *baccha ahimaṇṇo, pekkha paṅgaṇe raṅgiṇī taha taṇḍavio bi sihaṇḍī ciṭṭha{i} \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Enters with her son) My child Abhimanyu, look! Raṅginī and Tāṇḍavika the peacock are standing in the yard.**

***abhimanyuḥ:*** *amba saccaṁ kahesi \| jaṁ diṭṭhaṁ mae goba-maṇḍaleṇa saddhaṁ ekko jjebba goulaṁ pa{i}ṭṭho \|*

**Перевод:**

**Mother, you speak the truth for I have seen Balarāma enter Gokula alone with the cowherd boys.**

***jaṭilā:*** *baccha, esā visāriṇī kābi sorabbha-dhārā jjebba taṁ sāhasia-mihuṇam ettha kahei \|*

**Перевод:**

**Child, this perfume spreading everywhere indicates their adultery.**

***abhimanyuḥ:*** *amba bhavadīe nideso bi mae paḍipālido ajja saṁbutto \| tā dāṇīṁ rāhiaṁ mahurā-pure ṇa{i}ssaṁ \|*

**Перевод:**

**Mother, following Paurṇamāsī’s instruction is finished today. Now I will take Rādhikā to Mathurā.**

***jaṭilā:*** *putta diṭṭhiā ekka-duāraṁ gharaṁ \| tā duāra-bhittie laggā bhavia suṇahma patthāvam\| (iti tathā sthitau \|)*

**Перевод:**

**By luck, the room has one door. Staying at the threshold, let us listen to their conversation. (They go to the door.)**

*(praviśya)* ***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(sa-smitam): rādhe! mā sma kārṣīr atidurlabhe’sminn arthe prārthanam\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Smiling) Rādhā, do not request what is most difficult.**

***rādhikā*** *(sa-narma-smitam): a{i} dei pasīda pasīda \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Joyful smile) O goddess, be merciful, be merciful.**

***abhimanyuḥ*** *(gṛhaṁ praviśya): huṁ sāhasiṇi, paccakkham hatthāhatthi gahīdāsi \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Entering the room) Agh! Rash woman! I have caught you in the act.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(sva-gatam):* *hanta! svarād abhimanyum abhijñāya kātareyaṁ priyā yaṣṭivad bhūmau nipapāta \|*

**Перевод:**

**(To himself) Oh! Understanding Abhimanyu’s presence from his voice, my beloved has fallen in pain on the ground like stick.**

***jaṭilā*** *(sa-vismayam aṅgulyā darśayantī): baccha loottareṇa lāaṇṇa-jhāreṇa kā esā gaurī gharaṁ ujjalei?*

**Перевод:**

**(Pointing in astonishment) Who is this golden girl who illuminates the room with extraordinary beauty?**

***abhimanyuḥ*** *(vimṛṣya): amba dei pasīda pasīda tti bhaṇia rāhīe daṇḍa-ppaṇāmo kidatthi \| tā esā dibba-rūpā mahesa-mahisī phuḍaṁ pādubbhūdā \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Considering) Rādhā said, “O goddess, be merciful, be merciful.” She then offered respects on the ground. Śiva’s wife Durgā has clearly appeared.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(sa-harṣam ātma-gatam): gaurī-nepathyaṁ mama suṣṭhu pathyaṁ babhūva \|*

**Перевод:**

**(In joy to himself) My disguise as a golden girl has been very useful.**

***sakhyau*** *(sānandam): gobuttama tuhmāṇaṁ ammeḍideṇa ahmehiṁ ārāhijjantī gaurī paḍimādo ṇikkamidā \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Joyfully) Best of the cowherds, according to your words, we worshipped Durgā and she merged from the deity.**

***abhimanyuḥ:*** *bisāhe, kiṁ dāṇīṁ dei-pāde sudullahaṁ rāhie abbhatthidaṁ \|*

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā, what rare thing did Rādhā request at the feet of Durgā just now?**

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *vīrābhimanyo! dāruṇaṁ kim api saṅkaṭaṁ tavopasthitam \| tan nivṛttim iyaṁ yācate\|*

**Перевод:**

**Hero Abhimanyu! You have come upon great disaster. She has requested that this be eliminated.**

***abhimanyuḥ*** *(sa-saṅkam): bhaavadi, kerisaṁ tam?*

**Перевод:**

**O goddess, what is that calamity?**

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *vṛnde, tad-abhivyaktaye saṅkucanti me vacanāni \| tatas tvaya kathyatām \|*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā, my words shrink from revealing it. Therefore you should say.**

***vṛndā:*** *māninn abhimanyo, paraśvas tvaṁ bhojeśvareṇa bhairavāya sāyam upahārī-kartavyo’si \|*

**Перевод:**

**Proud Abhimanyu! The day after tomorrow, Kaṁsa will offer you as a human sacrifice in the evening to Śiva.**

***jaṭilā*** *(sa-vaiklavyam): dei! pasīda pasīda \| jīa-puttiaṁ maṁ karehi \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Feebly) O goddess, be merciful, be merciful. Save the life of my son.**

***rādhikā*** *(sa-harṣam utthāya): dei, pasīda pasīda \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Sands up in joy) Goddess, be merciful.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(smitvā): rādhe, varṇitam eva te \| yad adya dūrnivāram idam \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Smiling) O Rādhā, I have told you. Now it will be difficult to avoid.**

***rādhikā*** *(sa-kāku-bharaṁ praṇamya): hanta ballavī-ula-deade, kiṁ pi asakkam de ṇatthi \| tā tue ṇāheṇa abippaoaṁ pasādīkadua aṇugehīadu eso jaṇo \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Bows in a pitiful state) O goddess of all the cowherd women! What is impossible for you? Be merciful to this person, so that I am not separated from my master.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(smitvā):*  
*vaśī-kṛtātmāsmi vaśīndra-duṣkarais*  
*tavādya rādhe nava-bhakti-dāmabhiḥ \|*  
*tad-iṣṭa-siddhiṁ kṛta-gokula-sthitiḥ*  
*sadā mad-ārādhanatas tvam āpsyasi \|\|57\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Smiling) O Rādhā, today I have been controlled by your ropes of fresh devotion, difficult even for persons who control their senses. Staying in Gokula, you will attain your desired goal by worshipping me constantly.**

***abhimanyuḥ*** *(socchvāsam): a{i} bhatta-jaṇa-bacchale! kadā bi mahurāhimuhī mae ṇa rāhiā kādabbā \| tā iha basantī tumam esā ārāhedu \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Breathing in relief) O goddess, affectionate to your devotees! I will never take Rādhā to Mathurā. Let her worship you while staying here.**

***jaṭilā*** *(rādhām aliṅgya): a{i} goula-ṇandiṇi rakkhidahmi \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Embracing Rādhā) O joy of Gokula, I have been saved.**

***vṛndā*** *(abhimanyum avekṣya):*  
*vidhvaṁsayati hi puṁsāṁ*  
*sādhvī parivāditāyūṁṣi \|*  
*para-devatātra gaurī*  
*bhāva-grāhiṇy asau vadatu \|\|58\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Looking at Abhimanyu) A chaste woman who has been accused destroys the life span of men. Durgā is the supreme deity. She understands the truth. Let her speak.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *dhanyābhimanyo! kalyāṇa-sādhikā te rādhikā \| tad asyāṁ nāviśrabdhena bhavitavyaṁ bhavatā \|*

**Перевод:**

**Fortunate Abhimanyu! Rādhikā gives you good luck! You should never lose faith in her.**

***abhimanyuḥ:*** *dei rāhī-vesaṁ kadua subaleṇa ambā me parihasijja{i} \| taṁ pekkhia maccharī aṇahiṇṇo micchā-hisattiṁ uppābedi \|*

**Перевод:**

**O goddess! Dressing up like Rādhā, Subala made fun of my mother. Seeing that, some ignorant, hateful people defame her.**

***lalitā:*** *ahimaṇṇo diṭṭhiā saaṁ ccea bīsatthosi \|*

**Перевод:**

**Abhimanyu, by good fortune, you have personally developed faith.**

***abhimanyuḥ:*** *amba ehi maha gharam \| sabbassa{i}ṁ mahura-pure ṇeduṁ ṇijuttaṁ janaṁ ṇivarehma \| (ity ambayā saha hariṁ praṇamya niṣkrāntaḥ \|)*

**Перевод:**

**Mother, go to my house. Stop the people engaged in taking my valuables to Mathurā. (Offering respects to Kṛṣṇa disguised, he exits with his mother.)**

***sakhyau*** *(rādhām āśliṣya sāsram): ha pia-sahi! kadhaṁ pāmarehiṁ tumaṁ mahurā-pure ṇeduṁ ṇiccidāsī \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Embracing Rādhā with tears) O dear friend! Why are these fools determined to take you to Mathurā?**

*(praviśya)* ***paurṇamāsī*** *(sānanda-smitam):*  
*aṅgarāgeṇa gaurāṅgī*  
*hiraṇya-dyuti-hāriṇī \|*  
*mām agre rañjayaty eṣā*  
*nikuñja-kula-devatā \|\|59\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(Entering, she speaks with a blissful smile.) This golden girl in front of me, the goddess of all the bowers, whose complexion surpasses gold, attracts me by her scented cosmetics.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(parikramya): bhagavati vande \|*

**Перевод:**

**(Approaching her) I offer respects to Paurṇamāsī.**

***paurṇamāsī:*** *āśīḥ-satam \| hanta yasoda-mataḥ! diṣṭyā bhavatādya saṁvardhitāsmi yad aham rādhikā-viśleṣa-vedanānām anabhijñīkṛta \|*

**Перевод:**

**I give a hundred blessings to you. O son of Yaśodā! Today you have brought me to maturity since I no longer know the pain of Rādhā in separation.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  
*uttīrṇā parama-bhayād babhūva rādhā*  
*nirbādhājani jaratī gatādhī śūciḥ \|*  
*niḥśaṅkaṁ pramadam itās tathādya sakhyaḥ*  
*kartavyaṁ bhagavati kiṁ priyaṁ tavāsti \|\|60\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā has been delivered from the greatest fear. You, shining brightly, have been relieved of all anxiety. Today your friends have joy without anxiety. What more can I do to please you, Paurṇamāsī?**

***paurṇamāsī*** *(sānandāsram): gokula-bandho! bāḍham avandhya-janmāsmi kṛtā \| tathāpi kiñcid abhyarthaye—*  
*prathayan guṇa-vṛndā-mādhurīm*  
*adhi-vṛndāvana-kuñja-kandaram \|*  
*saha rādhikayā bhavān sadā*  
*śubham abhyasyatu keli-vibhramam \|\|61\|\|*

*kim ca—*  
*antaḥ-kandalitādaraḥ śruti-puṭīm ugdhāṭayan sevate*  
*yas te gokula-keli-nirmala-sudhā-sindhūttha-bindum api \|*  
*rādhā-mādhavikā-madhor mādhurīmā svārājyam asyārjayan*  
*sādhīyānbhavadīya-pāda-kamale premormir unmīlatu \|\|62\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**(In bliss) O friend of Gokula! My birth has been made most fruitful. I make this request. Along with Rādhikā may you always reign over the beautiful recesses of the Vṛndāvana bowers, busily engaged in pastimes, while spreading sweetness with all qualities!**

**And, may there arise excellent waves of prema for your lotus feet! That prema, sprouting reverence in the heart, opening the ears, serving even a drop of pure nectar arising from the pastimes in Gokula, earns the sweet kingdom of honey from the** ***mādhavī*** **creeper of Rādhā.**

***kṛṣṇaḥ*** *(smitvā): bhagavati tathāstu \| tad ehi \| go-dohāvasāne mām aprekṣya cintayiṣyantau pitarāv avilambaṁ gokulaṁ praviśya nandayāvaḥ \| (iti niṣkrāntaḥ \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(Smiling) O Paurṇamāsī so be it. Go. My parents are worried, not seeing me at the time of milking the cows. Hurrying to Gokula, we two will make them happy. (Exits)**

*(iti niṣkrāntāḥ sarve \|)*

**Перевод:**

**(All exit.)**

## Blessings: grantha-samaptiḥ

*rādhā-vilāsaṁ vītāṅkaṁ*  
*catuḥ-ṣaṣṭi-kalā-dharam \|*  
*vidagdha-mādhavaṁ sādhu*  
*śīlayantu vicakṣaṇāḥ \|\|1\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**May the intelligent study well** ***Vidagdha-mādhava*****, a drama about pastimes of Rādhā, who is endowed with the sixty-four arts.**

*nanda-sindhu-bāṇendu-*  
*saṅkhye saṁvatsare gate \|*  
*vidagdha-mādhavaṁ nāma*  
*nāṭakaṁ gokule kṛtam \|\|2\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This drama called** ***Vidagdha-mādhava*** **was completed in Gokula in 1533 AD.**

*śānta-śriyaḥ parama-bhāgavatāḥ samantād*  
*vaiguṇya-puñjam api sad-guṇatāṁ nayanti \|*  
*doṣāvalim aparitāpitayā mṛdūni*  
*jyotīṁṣi viṣṇu-pada-bhāñji vibhūṣayanti \|\|3\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The most exalted devotees, endowed with a wealth of peace, turn the abundant faults of the book into good qualities, just as softly shining constellations situated in the sky decorate all the evenings without giving painful heat.**

## References

May this literary jewel written by and revealing the qualities of Rūpa Gosvāmī, brilliant with service to the Lord of Vṛndāvana, decorate the cavity of my heart. May it quickly bestow to me the mercy of his glance!

The sugar candy of the Lord’s pastimes should completely remove the thirst of the living entities in the form of the path of saṁsāra, which forces the jīvas into bodies of devatā, men and trees and thus produces mental and physical suffering. That sugar candy of the Lord’s pastimes defeats the pride of the nectar emanating from the moon, which thinks it has the sweetest nectar, and distributes its fragrance or attractiveness through the camphor of Rādhā’s love. *Surabhi* can mean fragrance or attractive according to Viśva.

The author gives the benediction for the manifestation of Mahāprabhu, the most worshipable deity, by whose inspiration he had obtained the ability to produce this extraordinary drama, since the taste for the Lord’s pastimes cannot arise without a manifestation of Lord Caitanya. May the lord or lion (harih), son of Śacī, appear in the cavity of your hearts. He has appeared in Kali-yuga to offer mercifully the wealth of his own worship, which was not offered before. ”Did not Kapila teach worship of the Lord to his mother?” Lord Caitanya has offered wealth in the form of his worship which is profuse *mādhurya-rasa.* Kapila did not teach bhakti witih a predominance of *mādhurya-rasa*. The Lord shines more beautifully than a mass of gold. As a lion appears in a mountain cave and kills an elephant residing there, may Lord Caitanya appear in your hearts and kill all inauspiciousness residing there.

After two introductory verses of blessings (called *nāndī),* the director of the performance (*sūtradhara*) speaks. This is Rūpa Gosvāmī. Let the two verses be sufficient introduction. In all dramas the director makes this statement in order to increase longing in the audience. The characteristics of a *nāndī* are given as follows:

prastāvanāyāṁ tu mukhe nāndī kāryā śubhāvahā \|\|

āśīrnamaskriyā-vastunirdeśānyatamānvitā \|\|15\|\|

aṣṭabhir daśabhir yuktā kiṁ vā dvādaśabhiḥ padaiḥ \|\|

candranāmāṅkitā prāyo maṅgalārtha-padojjvalā \|

maṅgalaṁ cakra-kamala-cakrora-kumudādikam \|\|16\|\|

In the introductory dialogue at the beginning (*prastāvanā*), the *nāndī,* invoking auspiciousness, indicates the purpose of the work as well as giving blessings and offering respects, using eight, ten or twelve half lines of verse. It contains pure words for auspiciousness such as “moon.” A wheel, lotus, a cakora bird and a waterlily are auspicious. The purpose of this work will be to reveal the s*ambhoga* (union) of Rādhā and Mādhava. The first verse is an example of a twelve pada *nāndī.*

The Śiva mentioned in this verse is Gopīśvara Śiva who lives on the bank of Brahma-kuṇḍa. Śiva’s order heard in a dream is explained. “O Rūpa, skilful at dramatic arts! Persons whose hearts are intensely attracted by Kṛṣṇa’s prema, intimately involved in *rasa (rasika-sampradayaḥ),* have come near Keśī-tīrtha from various places with desire to see Vṛndāvana. It is said:

līlā-premṇā priyādhikyaṁ mādhuryaṁ veṇu-rūpayoḥ \|

ity asādhāraṇaṁ proktaṁ govindasya catuṣṭayam \|\|

His extraordinary qualities are his special pastimes, his devotees endowed with abundant *prema*, the sweetness of his flute and the sweetness of his form. BRS 2.1.43

The four extraordinary qualities of Kṛṣṇa are described. The root is his devotees endowed with special prema *(priyādhikyam*). The other three qualities (his pastimes, flute and form) stem from this.

The devotees (*ballavī)* are thus described first by the director because of their incomparable prema. Kṛṣṇa is the ocean in which the host of gopīs’ minds play like *makarīs.* The sweetness of his flute is described. He is the first teach of those who have mastered study of perfect flute playing. The special nature of Vṛndāvana pastimes, superior to those of Mathurā and Dvārakā are described. He performs pastimes like an intoxicated cuckoo in the groves of trees *(kānana-lekhāyām)*, rich (*tuṇḍilāyām*) with beauty of fragrant flower clusters on the bank of the Yamunā. The sweetness of his form is described. He is the crest jewel of young boys (*kiśora*), courageous as a new cloud on the slopes of Govardhana, which increase the highest bliss. This suggests that *kaiśora* form is eternally youthful.

These devotees who have gathered here are fortunate. At this place previously the *gopīs* searched for Kṛṣṇa. Here on the bank of the Yamunā they experienced great joy (*rabhasaḥ*) from the *rāsa* dance. Hearing many such pastimes, these devotees roll on the ground. Somehow they pass their days in separation from Kṛṣṇa.

Therefore now since you can produce a river of sweet pastimes, you must maintain their *prāṇas* which have come outside their bodies because of the *uddīpanas* which stimulate pain in separation from Kṛṣṇa. My mercy will enable you complete producing a work on Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. *Samagram* means all or complete according to *Amara-koṣa.* I will carry out Śiva’s order.

The assistant director enters among the gathered devotees and speaks. This persons moves all around on the sides (p*āripārśvaka*). He is a character in the drama who acts as the student of the director. O learned sir (*bhāva*), who knows the emotions to be conveyed in drama! *Bhāva* means learned person or father according to *Amara-koṣa.* The characters (*bhūmikāḥ*) dressed as Rādhikā, Madhumaṅgala and others according to the necessities of the new play, these performers (*kuśīlavāḥ*) entreat your honored self to enter the stage.

The director then speaks. O dear friend (*māriśa*)! This is an address to his student, a lesser actor. Bharata says *mānyo* *bhāva iti jñeyaṁ kiṁcid ūnas tu māriṣaḥ: māriṣa* is used to address respectfully a slightly inferior person. Have you prepared all the ornaments and costumes for Rādhikā and others (*varṇikā-parigrahaḥ*) according the progress of the drama named *Vidagdha-mādhava*? After considering for a moment—realizing that all had been prepared, he says “Let it happen.”

In my play the poetry is not attractive but the intelligent will obtain joy because of a trace of Kṛṣṇa in the work. An example is given. What intelligent person would not drink with bowed head the water from a well whose water manifests (*udgara*) greatness by bathing a *śalagrāma.*

O master! Though we do not have skill in performing, we pray to the audience by praising the great skill in the drama. This means “You should mercifully accept the expertise in this drama.” This audience by their learning can criticize the *devatās.*

Such actions are useless (*kṛtam)* since your words refer uselessly to persons of different qualities. *Kṛtam* means Satya-yuga and accomplishment according to *Amara-koṣa.*

Just as good persons are embarrassed at the manifestation of desiring others’ wives or possessions devotees are embarrassed when persons glorify them. They are humble though they possess education, wealth and good families or if they are beautiful. The more they have education etc. the more they become humble.

Being overwhelmed, he calls out “O devotees of Kṛṣṇa, the best of the cowherds (*ballabha-siṁha*)! This actor, desiring to say something to you, who are the gurus in the assembly of persons who know about devotion, is now ascended the podium of the shameless (*nirapatrapāṇām)*. Please forgive his bold insolence.” The director then looks while offering respects.

This work will fulfill (*vidhātrī)* all of your (*vaḥ*) desires (*siddhārthān*). The work has appeared from me, low by nature (*prakṛti-laghu-rūpāt*). The word *rūpa* also indicates Rūpa Gosvāmī. Sarasvatī, unable to tolerate his humility, gives another meaning to the words. *Prakrṭi-laghu-rūpāt* means “he who quickly *(laghu*) produces (*rūpāt*) an excellent work (*pra—kṛti*).

May Kṛṣṇa whose mind shines in the pastimes of Vraja bestow satisfaction to me. Or, may he whose mind is absorbed in Kṛṣṇa (*vraja-vihāra*) or he whose mind is absorbed in Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa who perform pastimes in Vraja, whose body is called Sanātana, bestow satisfaction to me. He (Kṛṣṇa or Sanātana) shows sweet mercy to the surrendered souls (*prapanna-madhurodayaḥ*). Or he produces (*prapanna udayaḥ*) *śṛṅgāra-rasa* *(madhura*).

These devotees have unimpeded intelligence (*anargala-dhiyām*). This indicates the special qualification of the recipients. The work named *Vidagdha-mādhava* concerning Kṛṣṇa, friend of the gopīs, is permeated (*pallavitaḥ*) with ornaments in the form of spontaneous statements. This indicates the special nature of the work. The land within Vṛndāvana has become the arena (*catvaratām)* for the performance of the drama. This indicates the special nature of the place. The special time is indicated in the next verse. Therefore (*tat*) hasten to distribute the sweetness of rasa, O director! It is not proper to delay since the time, place and objects are proper.

O assistant! I worry because of the opposition of persons without rasa present in the assembly of qualified persons who will hear this, since this play is filled with descriptions of *parakīya-rasa* which cause Jaṭilā and others to harshly chastise Kṛṣṇa.

It is very clear (*nāma)* that those ignorant of rasa will be indifferent to everything. Those who appreciate rasa will praise (*sphuranti)* this new dramatic work (*kṛtau*). The cuckoo rejoices only (*param*) in the mango which is completely (*kāmam*) ignored by camels (*kramelakaiḥ*).

In the spring, the moon (*īśvaram*) has become newly red in response (*anurāga*). Or Kṛṣṇa has attained new *anurāga.* The full moon night with the nine planets (*guḍhāgrahāḥ*) or which has deep longing meets with beautiful (*rucirayā*) Viśākhā constellation (*rādhayā*). Rucira means “that which accepts (*ra*) beauty (*ruci*).” Or Kṛṣṇa meets with Rādhā. Rādhā means Viśākhā constellation according to *Amara-koṣa.* In the full moon of Vaiśākha month Viśākhā constellation becomes prominent for creating beauty (*raṅgāya).* Paurṇamāsī (the tithi and the devotee) reveals confidential pastimes.

O director famous in all the worlds among all dramatists!

He faces the dressing room. *Nepathya* means the stage or dressing room according to *Viśva* \| *Uṭaja-vīthīṁ* means a street with thatched cottages.

*arthasya pratipādyasya tīrthaṁ prastāvanocyate: prastāvanā* means the place where the meaning is explained. Paurṇamāsī then enters and speaks.

The tree of good fortune has not been sufficiently nourished by suitable actions so that conjugal love cannot take place. But the couple should have that conjugal love.

O great devotee (*bhavādi*)! If Rādhikā is supposed to unite with Kṛṣṇa by your arrangement, why did she give up living in a place favorable for meeting and remain hidden in Bhānu-tīrtha, at a place called Santanu?

*Nṛśaṁsa* means cruel according to *Amara-koṣa*.

*Guṇa-śrīḥ* means a wealth of qualities.

The marriage was illusory. That which is permanent at all times is covered by *yogamāyā.* Real illusion is caused by *māyā* not *yogamāyā.* If *māyā* operated, there would be unreality in pastimes such as Kṛṣṇa eating dirt and showing the universal form.

Kṛṣṇa is the bee. \| Abhimanyu wants to take the golden lotus (*puraṭa-padminīm*) to Madhuvana or Madhurā (*vanāntram*). *Vana* also means water. Thus the meaning is also “He wants to take the lotus to other water.”

She remains indifferent in this case, and does not resolve the problem. Paurṇamāsī felt this way from seeing events when the couple tried to meet. It should be understood however that yogamāyā did not act at this time in order to increase rasa by producing worry and anger.

*Pratibhuvā* means surety.

The two syllables dancing in the mouth (*tanḍavini tuṇḍe*) make one desire to have many mouths. What is the use of one mouth? If I had many mouths I could happily chant Kṛṣṇa’s name. As soon as the syllables sprout in the ear (*karṇa-kroda-kaḍambini*) they produce the desire for millions of ears. Entering the courtyard of the heart, the syllables defeat the activities (*kṛtim*) of the senses. The senses becomes subservient because of great bliss. The active verb *ji* means to defeat. As an intransitive verb it means “to remain with excellence.”

*Ālekhyam* means drawing. *Praticchandam* means a drawn likeness.

*Jāguḍam* means *kuṁkuma.* *Pariṣkriyā* means ornaments. *Hariṇ-maṇiḥ* means an emerald according to *Amara-koṣa.* His ornaments made of forest flowers and garlands (*araṇyajā*) defeat respect given to heavenly ornaments.

*bhaved viśkambhako bhāvi-bhūta-vastv-aṁśa-sūcakam*: *viśkambhaka* refers to an interlude in a drama briefly indicating future or past events. *Nāṭaka-candrika* 196

Crystal mountains play the role of cows. Mandākinī, Gaṅgā in the heavens, has fallen from Indra’s city. This I guess. The river’s purpose is to develop bliss from serving Yamunā in Vṛndāvana.

The land is spread closely (*sambādhi-kṛta*) nearby (*savidham*) with many fields spread (*vitatibiḥ*) with many spires. It is spread with the slopes of Govardhana. *Kaṭaka* means hips according to *Amara-koṣa.* The pastures extend up to Kāliya lake. They nourish or uphold increasing *(samṛddhām*) beauty.

*Bāliśo* means fool. *Saṁjñā* means to communicate using the hands according to *Amara-koṣa.*

*Jālma* means “being inconsiderate.”

*Kamala-gandheḥ* means “having the fragrance of a lotus.” The formation *gandhi* occurs in a compound. A*mbara-knopam* means “moistening her garment.”

*Candra-parāga* means camphor. *Nalada* refers to the root of the *vīraṇa* plant.

*Go-kadambam* means a group of cows. *Tatra-bhavantau* is a respectful form of address.

*Mākanda* means mango. *Tundilayati* means “increases.”

Objects with mutually opposing qualities give up their original natures and take on opposite natures.

*Deva-priyaḥ* means an animal.

*Nava* means new. *Śatdhṛti* is Brahmā. *Marāla* is a swan.

Nārada sings.

*Parimalān* means a concentrated perfume. *Deva-mātṛka* means “nourished by rain water.”

*Mādhavīyām* means related to the spring or to Kṛṣṇa.

Progressively, the ear, skin, eye, nose, and tongue are stimulated.

Using his friend as a pretext, he requests for himself, showing his boldness.

*Rasajña* can mean tasty or “for a person who has good taste.”

*Parināyitā* means to be completely led or married. Because of this, these actions have been ordered. The marriage was arraned by Mukharā (*etayā)*.

*Vilakṣa* means filled with surprise according to *Amara*-*koṣa.*

Seeing you wear a flower crown they develop desire to make crowns for themselves. The real meaning however is “they are overcome with love (*kusumeṣu-rāgaḥ*).

O Paurṇamāsī with hair white as a line of cranes. This has come about by old age. This indicates her skill in having her desires fulfilled. You do not give up assisting the offenders. Or you do not give up assisting those whom Rādhā has left.

How can the gopīs with Rādhā be those whom Rādhā left? They boldly steal the heart of the best of males (*punnāgasya*).

The other meaning is “I was talking about the attractive *sakhī*.”

The other meaning is “I do not exist in Rādhā’s group, bewildering to a lover. I see that you however exist there.”

What is the use of hankering for Viśākhā constellation? The real meaning is “What is the use of hankering after Rādhā? She is eternally yours.” How can a human attain the constellation which moves in the path in the sky (*viṣṇu-pada*)? The other meaning is “How can a human like Abhimanyu obtain Rādhā, who enacts pastimes with you (*viṣṇu-pada)* alone?”

*Aṣṭa-padam* means gold.

*halā* means friend.

Your body is the abode of beauty incomparable in the worlds. O girl whose eyes are restless to go to the forest for *guñja* berries with great desire.

*Paṅkilayanti* means “turned to mud.” *Tāṇḍavayanti* means appears to be dancing. A causative verb is formed from that adjective. *Danturayanti* means to develop thorns. *Abhyāsam* means close.

*Vihaṅeśvaraḥ* means Garuḍa. *Kumbhodbhavaḥ* means Agastya.

If other people by chance hear such words uttered by Rādhā, they will think she is associating with some other man.

snehaḥ sa rāgo yena syāt sukhaṁ duḥkham api sphuṭam

tat-sambandha-lave ’py atra prītiḥ prāṇa-vyayair api \|\|

That *sneha* which turns sorrow into happiness by a slight appearance of Kṛṣṇa is called *rāga.* In this state of *rāga,* the devotee will do anything for the pleasure of Kṛṣṇa, even giving up his life. BRS 3.2.87

Is this some peculiar result of a dream, because of rashly worshipping at night? Or is it a pecular result in the waking state, caused by hasty worship during the day? In this way (*ittham*) in dream or waking, in night or day, I have become ignorant to really understand (*prakarṣeṇa jñātum*) since I have been inundated by many internal disturbances intensified by vibrations of the dark moon.

He had ointment of thick *kuṁkuma* spread with various patterns made by his fingers. *Priyakaḥ* means a *kadamba.*

I develop disturbances which become contrary, because of intense (*niviḍāt*) desire caused by the touch of his lotus hand. I do not know where I am. Kṛṣṇa is described. He has complexion like that of petals of slightly blossoming blue lotuses.

*Paṭīra-parvato* means Malaya mountain. *Saṁrambheṇa* means greatly. *Dhvani-bhṛto śilīmukhā* means bees. They will help me give up pain by causing me to faint. When the *prāṇas* are destroyed quickly by the *uddīpanas* such as the wind, I will no longer suffer.

Without stopping the pain, the doctor will only criticize me.

Tying the creeper around my neck you should have me give up my life.

*Gandha-phalī* means campaka flower. *Madhusūdanaḥ* is a bee. Your beauty is useless without being enjoyed by Kṛṣṇa.

You are a little beyond childhood (*dara-vicalita-bālyā*). Kṛsṇa is the cause of bewilderment (*mohanatvam*). Because of that bewildrement (*amunā*) you have become agitated.

Though they are qualified with dharma, including shyness, because of the strength of intense prema, that shyness has become lax. *Dharma-śailī* means practicing dharma. How is dharma trespassed with outright disrespect *(avinayotphullam*)? He is skilful in amorous arts revealed (*parimala*) in his glance.

*Navīna-grahaḥ* can also mean “he who is eager to enjoy young women.”

Her body like a forest (*vanī*) announces the victory of Kṛṣṇa, the young elephant on the bank of the Yamunā, situated in the bower of her mind. She tries to hide with great effort the noise. The feminine is used (*vaṇī)* to indicate the smallness of her body. The forest (her body) is sweet with the fragrance of fresh *mada* from the elephant’s forehead . Or her body is sweet with the fragrance of fresh musk *(mada*).

*Janāntikam* refers to talk between two people that others do not hear.

The word j*āgarti* (being awake) is used to indicate that prema always eixsts and never sleeps. This is its svarūpa*-lakṣaṇa*. All this is experienced (*jñāyate*) by a person, but cannot be described. A person’s *anurāga* will simply be perceived as a great burden. *Vakra-madhurāḥ* means that the path of sweetness is crooked. This is the meaning. This prema cannot be experienced by questions and answers. It is experienced only by great good fortune. If one becomes the shelter for suitable prema, then one will have experiences like pain from the piercing of thorns or a lance. One should strive for a similar *bhāva.*

O angry mother! You are angry at me without considering the circumstances. What can I say?

Friends cause misery. Why? We are simply minded. The plural is used to indicate that like Rādhā, her friends also are simple. How could they know that you are like this? You are the one who gives misery.

In *pūrva-rāga* the heroine through messenger, gives her lover love letters and garlands. Paurṇamāsī speaks according to *kāma-śāstra. Niviśya puṣpeṣu* also means “enter into love.”

*Naicikī* means cow according to *Amara-koṣa.*

*Yoginīva* means as if having attained brahman realization.Then he remembers the *raṅgaṇa* flower, since it is similar to Rādhā’s lips. Then he remembers that he has sent Madhumaṅgala for a *raṅgaṇa* garland.

The campaka creeper is golden (*tāpanīyam*) because of the full blossoms. *Niraṅka* means pure. *Tilaka* means best. Though all the women of Vraja are written in the heart of Kṛṣṇa, among them Rādhā has become the best *( tilakībabhūva*). I have never seen my friend absorbed in this way with any other person. Kṛṣṇa trembles on seeing the *campaka* which is similar to gold, which is similar to *kuṁkuma,* which is siimalr to Rādhā’s complexion. Oh, what intensity of his *raga!.*

gṛhāṇa \|

\|

My chest is thick as a mass of clouds *(mudirāli-medure*). She is fickle like lightning (*capalā*). I will be successful if I can touch her for a moment since she is fickle like lightning.

It is well known that the bee, sitting on his forehead, greedy for the scent of *mada*, agitates the elephant. \| This can be well inferred *(unnītam*).

My mind has been transformed from its natural state. *Saura-saindhavam* means “related to the Gaṅgā. *Sindhu* can refer to a river according to *Amara-koṣa*. More than in other months the full moon of Jyeṣṭha month causes the waves of the Gaṅgā to overflow its banks because of extreme disturbance.

My eyes are absorbed in her face with splendid beauty. Remembering the moon to compare it to her face, I feel hatred or disappointment and my mouth curls up in disgust.\|

Love experienced by great souls, not demons, because of natural qualities, not artificial ones, indicates a great person. The wandering of a black deer in a place indicates auspiciousness if it naturally lives there, not brought there by force.

Not completely satisfied with the sweetness of the words, he had Madhumaṅgala read the letter again to relish the words while pretending to be indifferent to the words. He wanted to convey that he was not immediately searching for her.

*Cāturakṣikaṁ* means “with two eyes meeting two eyes.” cāturakṣikam is declined like *adhyātmam.*

*Giri-grāmaṇīḥ* means the best of mountains. *Avakra* means incomprehensible.

Our hearts are marked with suffering but after death the suffering will remain. His smile makes us affectionate though he cheats us. Though he may love other women whose intelligence is blinded by love, you should not love him since you are intelligent. He is lusty for cowherd women and filled with deception.

This most charming Rādhā, endowed with great qualities, whose mouth chants the name of Kṛṣṇa, whose heart *(antarā*) is filled with prema (rāgena) spewing out, should rest on your throat like a *guñja* necklace. *Antara* can mean exterior according to *Amara-koṣa. Sāra adhikā* means with great excellence. It can also be *sā rādhikā*: this Rādhā.

When cannot be understood (*sukaṭhoram*) because they do not reveal themselves externally. They are crooked (*mālinyaṁ*) with pride *(udīrṇa*).

*Sāhasikyam* means to act rashly.

We cannot call them back now.

*Vidhure* means suffering. With the amin of attaining peace (*śāṇti-dhuram)* she may reject him. Her love *(mṛdvī) is* tender since it has just sprouted.

Her desire is to end her life.

She does not want to be separated from Rādhā.

*Sura-gireḥ* means “of Mount Sumeru.” It certainly cannot be (*hanta*) earth related to Ilāvṛta. Why do I see the directions colored yellow by the emanation of golden beauty? Certainy I understand it is not fixed land since I hear the loud sounds of ankle bells.

His chest is expert in blocking the strong river of my determination. When the strength of determination is gone, I become unsteady. His face as vowed to produce decrease in the lotus forest of chastity. *Yūpaḥ* is a pillar used to tie up animals to be used in sacrifices for casting spells against my elevated, long lasting shyness. The crooked snake of his eyes devours everything.

If we have been brought to this condition, is it right to be the object of your indifference? Your intention is only to kill us.

*Jīvātuḥ* means a life sustaining medicine according to *Amara-koṣa.*

I am only waiting for Lalitā. If I were not, I would give you association.

If Kṛṣṇa is without compassion, how have you offended me? Therefore do not uselessly weep. Funeral rights should not be done. Rather my body should remain fixed in Vṛndāvana, with its creeper arms on the shoulder of a *tamāla* tree.

According to the rule *antika-bāḍhayor nedasāṣye* (Pāṇini 5.3.63 ) *sādhiṣṭḥa* replaces *bādhā* in comparative and superlative expressions.

Longing to enter the Yamunā makes me hurry.

*Ammahe* can indicate astonishment or can be an address to another woman.

I will now give up my life.

O boy with new ointment! You intentionally attract their minds by wearing guñja necklaces and attract their eyes by wearing peacock feathers.

Her eyes move about because of bees wandering around me. Her eyes are endowed with eye lashes.

These items are for worshipping the sun.

The autumn cloud is Jaṭilā.

Who would not be attracted to the beauty of Rādhā with marks indicating great fame? Rādhā is equal to Lakṣmī or has great beauty. He is astonishing for her extraordinary attachment to me.

*Mānyām* means worthy of respect. It also means *mā anyām* (she belongs to no one else, only to me.)

*Kuraṅgam* can mean a deer of a person with ugly pastimes (*ku-raṅgam*).

*Sumanorāgeṇa* means redness of the flower. It also means prema of the pure mind. This redness or prema carries beauty (*kāntiṁ vahatā).* When you have flowered the parrot will not be indifferent to you. What to speak of when you bear fruit.

She understands Kṛṣṇa’s intentions to some degree.

Rādhā is dependent on Paurṇamāsī.

In the first two acts, explanations of the meanings were given with derivation of words. In the rest of the drama, only irregularities are explained.

At both ends (*ubhayataḥ*), extending for three fingers, the flute is studded with sapphires. The ends are covered with rubies. The middle portion is made of gold with diamonds.

When Madhumaṅgala told me to take the garland from Rādhā’s neck, could I then not have had her in my arms? He asked me this. Though she was close, she suddenly would turn away (*paraṅmukh*ī) out of shyness (*trapayā)*. Then taking the edge of the smiling friend’s garment, I would have made her come close to me. He repented in this way in his mind.

*Citta-cañcarīkam* means bee of the heart.

*Maṇisaraḥ* means a necklace.

*Vicakilair klptam* means “made of jasmine flowers.” Who is the woman on whom Kṛṣṇa has attained fixed his meditation?

*Nayopapannaḥ* means “endowed with proper bevaviour.”

What is the question of being passionately mad after Rādhā? (My comment)

*Vācāṭaḥ* means one who speaks profusely and vulgarly according to *Amara-koṣa.*

\[129\] *Aṅga-rāga* refers to an ointment made of musk, *kuṁkuma* and other substances.

O *kaṁsa-nisūdana*! Today the clothing in their bodies and conditions of the women’s houses have become loose because of you, when you play your flute whimsically (*līlayā*). *Vāsas* in the neuter means cloth. In the masculine it means a state or condition. That cloth spreads great beauty by taking shelter of the group of beautiful gopīs. The conditions of the houses spread great attractiveness by taking shelter of the group of chaste and noble women endowed with wealth and grains. Those clothes are filled with variety of enjoyment because of their various colors like white, blue and red garlands, sandalwood etc. The conditions in the houses have variety because of various types of structures and rooms. The clothes are the shelter of beauty because of various splendid materials. The conditions in the houses are the shelter of wealth according to various periods *(daśā*) of auspicious planets like Venus as described in scriptures.

It is hinted that since it is actually impossible to kill greatly powerful demons by mantras or potions, it is simply Kṛṣṇa’s nature to steal the minds of women.

How can her prema exist in his heart which is like a thunderbolt?

He speaks in anger in reply to Lalitā’s suggestive question, while giving up suggestive statements since this would reveal contrary qualities in the drama since, even though he is a clown, by nature he is ultimately a brāhmaṇa.

O black ocean! The river of Rādhā attains you after giving up on the path far away the tree of her husband (*dhava-taroḥ*) who was close by.It is well know that were there is *dhava* tree the river does not flow. *Dhava* also means husband. She is most excited in breaking the dam of dharma. She surpasses elders hard as mountains or huge mountains with great speed. *Nava-rasa* means fresh water, since flowing water does not become stale. Like the ocean with waves (*vīcibhiḥ),* why do you turn away from her with words (v*īcibhiḥ*), by producing newly concocted scriptures?

By your words I understand that you do not understand his unfavorable actions. Recognizing his state of mind you should quickly think of some method so that they can meet.

She is just a child. Friends make effort to block the door *(pratihāra*). The moon stone turns into water drops in the moon light.

Not responding to persons with love produces sins with sharp results (*kaṭu-pākāni*). *Nikhilam* means all of Gokula. The evening is Rādhā. The sun is Kṛṣṇa. *Hi* indicates everything.

Viṣṇu’s right eye is the sun. Closing the eye indicates that it is evening. *Heli-bimbe* means “image of the sun.”

Coconut water mixed with camphor produces poison according medical scriptures.

*Nalakinīm* means lotus. *Atanuḥ* means Cupid. He is compared to a wild elephant. *Kṣuṇatti* means crushes. If I live till sunrise, then the sun will reveal him. But that is not possible. It is hinted “Think of a method so that I can meet him right now.”

Just a little fire can burn up the *raṅgaṇa* creeper. The remedy for fire which reaches the clouds cannot be a few pots of water but rather dark clouds. If they shower plenty of rain, the fire can be extinguished. There is no other way.

O friend affectionate to me (*mayi snigdhe*)! Todyay in the evening since I cannot tolerate delay, I should becomea bee, suitable to enjoy the garland. You should bless me to give up my life. With this in mind he words express her determination to give up her life.

O Rādhā with beautiful body (*sutanu)!* How can I praise (*abhistuve*) the results of your karma? *Tanuḥ* means thin.

Why does one of the two does not come? In this way he shows longing. *Taraṅgiṇī-rati-guroḥ* means the ocean. *Ghūka* means owl. The night produces powedered ointment for their eyes and bewilders elephants.

*Haritam* means direction.

*Cañcala-pañcaśākhā* means fickle hands. *Pañcaśākhaḥ* means hands according to *Amara-koṣa.* This is an explanation of the name Viśākhā. However Viśākhā can also mean “having no branches.” Thus describing her as having five branches is a contradiction. Rādhā and Viśākhā also names of the same constellation.\|

*Tūṣṇīṁ* means silent according to *Amara-koṣa.*

*Sarasvatī* means words. *Bhartṛ-dāraka* means a prince.

*Vartmanīm* means a path.

The beauty of the moon, lotus and stars cannot compare to Rādhā’s beauty.

*Agham* means sin according to *Amara koṣa. Anagha-karmā* means a person who performs activities without suffering.

The crest jewel of Garuḍa can refer to Garuḍa, chief of the snakes or to Kṛṣṇa, who rides Garuḍa.

Desiring protection, you must enter among her pearls and become the center jewel on the mountainous breasts of Rādhā. Those pearls which are present over her heart (*sahṛdayā*), because of being strung on the best thread (*sād-guṇyād*), serve the person who has broad arms (*pṛthula-doṣa puruṣam*).

*Sajjati* means no words come from the mouth.\|

Quickly touching it, it remains close by. I am in that condition.

The eastern direction (*prācī*) whose husband is Indra, kisses the moon. He points to the east. The east is like you and I am like the moon. Therefore our situation is proper.

*Avikala-srī* can mean unimpaired wealth or incomprehensible grace.

*Ullāghaya* means to make free of sickness, according to *Amara-koṣa.*

*Śilīmukhau* means a bee or an arrow according to *Amara-koṣa.* Take shelter of the forests endowed with arrows. Since it is hard to enter, it can protect you. The other meaning is a garland black because of bees on it.

*Ahīna* means broad or Kaḷiya snake. *Khara* means sharp or the donkey Dhenuka. *Pralamba* means hanging down or the demon of that name. *Balaṁ* means strength or Balarāma. I am conquered by those enemies who were conquered by me or those qualities present in you. Your strength is impossible to understand.

*Cāmīkara* means gold. *Sammīra-preṅkholāt* means “swinging in the breeaze.” *Yena* means “because of the tamāla tree.”

Seeing the abode named after liberated persons and desiring to attain that place, giving up attachment to all friends, I attain oneness. I have attained qualification for uniting with your breasts. Those breasts do not give shelter to an uneven sesame seed: they do not have the slightest unevenness (*tilam-vaiṣamyam*). Or the breasts do not show any prejudice, being qualified as the Supreme Lord. The breasts are most beautiful, being shaped symmetrically. Satisfy me by the joyful bestowal of oneness in the form a flow of thick nectar.

*Apāṁ patyuḥ* means the ocean. *Rasa-pākaḥ* means an elixir. The moon is a priest in a sacrifice for killing all the *cakrāvaka* birds. *Vicarāvaḥ* means “Let us go.”

It is said that two parties, one favorable the other unfavorable, produce rasa. The rasa generated by the favorable party has been described with *pūrva-rāga* and meeting. Now rasa mixed with the opposing party is introduced. A pastime on the fourth day after Vaiśkākha full moon is now described.

Even his fragrance is unavailable in the southern portions of the pastures. This means that on the bank of the Yamunā in a bower of *karṇikāra* flowers, in a hut near Mukharā’s house in the northern portion of the pastures resides Rādhā. This is where he spreads his beauty. He does not seek out any other person. This is what you have hinted. But how is this possible? ---I have seen the following. *Bimbitā* means attached. The garlands in Śyāmā’s garlands quiver from Kṛṣṇa’s embraces. *Mañju* means attractive. It is possible that he has gone to the *bakula* groves on the side of Govardhana, near the southern pastures today since I have seen Kṛṣṇa enjoying persons in Candrāvalī’s group such as Śaibyā. Why do you hint that he is only attracted to Rādhā and not to Candrāvalī, since Śaibyā is in her group, Śyāmā is in a neutral group, and Bhadrā in a friendly group? Since there is no competition among them, they are not contrary to Candrāvalī. She does not mention Rādhā, Lalitā or Viśākhā since they are in the enemy group.

Vṛndā speaks. With the flute in his left hand he walks. The flute is the foremost abode of complete music of song in all the worlds. Everywhere else is devoid of song.

This is Candrāvalī’s grandmother.

Sakhīsthalī is Candrāvali’s village, known as Sakhītharā*. Upaśalyaṁ abhajat* means “He has arrived close.”

*Taraṇeḥ* means “of the sun.”

*Laghu* means small.

*Yāto yāma* means “time being wasted.” Though I have wasted time, the time is not wasted. Though the nights pass, they do not pass in sorrow.

*Madhura* means *śṛṅgāra-rasa* and sweet.

The other meaning is “Because of uttering two attractive syllables my ears my ears are filled with beauty.”

*Cakita-sāraṅga-nayane* means “a woman with eyes of a frightened doe.” Gold ornaments are in your ears. The emanation of sweet syllables from your mouth agitates my two ears. The word *suvarṇa* indicates words of longing. “Outside the moon-like face” also means the cheeks. They become beautiful. The reflections of gold from the earrings spread to your cheeks which become beautiful and agitate my eyes.

*Soḍaśa-kalasya* means “of the moon.” *Soḍaśī* also refers to the constellation Viśākhā also called Rādhā. How is it possible for the constellation in the sky to meet me, situated on earth? The real meaning is “She is known to me in another form.”

This also refers to the constellation named Rādhā.\|

Seeing the moon-like cheeks in the lotus face of Candrāvalī, I do not experience happiness. The cheeks have been contaminated by false arguments. I am overcome with worries and my heard is trembling in misery.

A*vaskanditām* means covered. The darkness is Candrāvalī’s anger.

*Kulyā* means a canal according to *Amara-koṣa. Pramadam* means bliss. *Keśara* is the *bakula* tree. The grove has plentiful water (*nīrādhikā*).

When she moves her brows, Cupid’s bow becomes useless. She is a box of jewels of sweetness. Kṛṣṇa refers to Rādhā but Padmā and Madhumaṅgala think he is referring to Candrāvalī.

He thinks of Rādhā and they think he speaks of Candrāvalī. She defeats the full moon with the beauty of her face.

He has said the name of Candrāvalī. What is the doubt?

The two do not hear this, since they have gone far off.

He interprets Kṛṣṇa’s words as *maṅgala-bhāra- adhika* instead of *maṅgala-bhā* Rādhikā.

With astonishment he speaks.

The six qualities are peace, war, marching, sitting, dividing and giving shelter. S*āḍguṇya* comes for *ṣaḍguṇa.* It is formed like *cāturvarṇya*. Among the six qualities, he who is engaged in war and peace is great. Therefore it is not difficult for you to make peace.

Seeing the couple eager for pastimes, Padmā uses this pretext to drag Madhumaṅgala away.

She should have placed the necklace on her breasts. The mascara should have been put on her eyes.

Applying dark ointment, they go for appointments. You are however the opposite. The effulgence of your body lights up the intense darkness. Your effulgence emerges through the small holes in the dark fabric. The intense darkness has become most bright. O enemy of yourself! This is a praise of Rādhā’s beauty, that cannot be compared, by scolding.

*Tamī-yāma* refers to the fourth lunar day after the Vaiśākha full moon. Paulomī is Śacī the wife of Indra. The moon satisfies completely the eastern direction by showing itself and by caressing with its hand. The moon meets its lover the eastern direction. It is however the enemy of other women who go for meetings. This is astonishing.

*Kauśakāḥ* means owls. *Auttānapādeḥ* is the pole star.

*Tāmbūla* is mixed with camphor (*ghanasāra*). Her necklace was strung with attractive emeralds. This is how the bower looked.

You are a teacher of worship of tantra using black magic.

*Āṭopam* means very audacious.

*Aratim* means pain.

The word *keśara* can indicate *bakula* according to the dictionary. Lalitā takes the meaning to be *bakula. Nāga-keśara* is also called *kāñcana* or *cāmpeya*. The word *keśara* indicates *nāga-keśara* by usage just as Bhīma means Bhīmasena and Satyā means Satyabhāmā. “This is the meaning I take. You take the word to mean *bakula*, and thus try to fool me. You are famous for doing this.”

*Nava-rasa-dhāriṇi* means fresh showers of water or the shelter of ninth rasa. *śānto'pi navamo rasaḥ: śānta* is the ninth rasa. (*Kāyva-prakāśa*) *Kṛṣṇa- mudire* means a black cloud or Kṛṣṇa who is like a cloud. *Kṣaṇa-rocisaḥ* means lightning or momentary beauty. The women in love show opposite symptoms out of disgust. *Gaurī* means refers to golden lighting or a golden women.

Instead of using sweet words they have make me suffer.

His pleasing form is decorated with a series of garlands. *Kaṭaka* means bracelet according to *Amara-koṣa.* He is decorated with minerals like *gairika* (red chalk).

*Tamī-sambhava-tamaḥ* means grief arising in the night.

He takes her words to mean “When she does not see his bower lit by the rays of cruel Candrāvalī.”

The sentence should be completed with “Even though Candrāvalī came.”

*Caṅga* means attractive.

His heart shines like a black stone. He spreads all contamination (*saṅkulayan kalaṅka-tatibhiḥ*).

You gaze at the path with no blinking within the eyes. Your eyes are red from the pollen not from staying awake with enjoyment at night. Your lips are wounded, not by the lover’s teeth, but from the cold wind.

*Kroḍa* means chest as well as Varāha. Prahlāda means great joy and the devotee Prahlāda.Nṛsimḥa elevated Prahlāda. Vāmana bound up Bali. *Bali-bandhaḥ* also means having three lines on the waist. Rāmā refers to Lakṣmī and the three Rāmas. You have intense beauty. *Śrī-ghana* also means Buddha. *Manasi kalkitā* means contamination in the mind, cruelty because of anger. Kalkī is the last *avatāra.*

*Vanyāntar-guru-cāpalam* also means „wandering a lot in the forest” *Go-saṅgati* aḷso means associating with cows. He is cruel, using his finger nails against the women. *Sucaṇḍī-mādhurā* means most intolerable and refers to Paraśurāma. *Ālaṅkeśa-vidhvaṁsanam* can mean he pulls the women’s hair. He gives suffering to us women (*iṣṭa-kadanam*). He holds a sword in play, or holds a sword like Kalki.

Viśākhā means without branches (*vigatā śākhā*).

*Vīthī* means path. Just as the Gaṅgā decorates Śiva’s head with a crescent moon, may the garland, by its movements, nourish the beauty of your breasts decorated nail marks like crescent moons.

O slender Rādhā! When he is surrendering with plaintive words (*kāku-parāyaṇe*) your contrary actions (*vāmyāni*) are not desirable. They have no value. The reason is given. When Kṛṣṇa becomes separated for a moment from the area of your lotus eyes, a second becomes like *manvantara* periods.
