He is like Indra, playing intently on the edge of a group of *kadamba* trees in the Vṛndāvana forest. He is compared to Indra becomes of his independent playing.

O Rādha, whose face is more beautiful than the moon!

He makes them give up their houses and become attached to the forest. He is expert in manifesting profusely many qualities of the rascals.

*Bindu-cyutake* is a type of literary ornament. You repeated do such acts (*naipuṇyam).* You have stolen my flute (*vaṁśī vaśīkṛtā*). *Vaṁśī* becomes *vaśī* when the bindu (m) is dropped. This is *bindu-cyutakālaṅkāra*.

*Kitava* means a cheater. *Pratibhuvaḥ* means guarantors.

She was the wife of the son of Yaśodā’s maternal uncle.

*Madhye dinam* means in the middle of the day. Mathurā is near (*kiyati pathi*). Going to Mathurā, I will inform Kaṁsa of everything and in that way produce peace. In this way she intimidates him.

After describing stealing the flute and manifestation of pride on the early morning of fifth *tith*i after Vaiśākha-full moon, events up to the afternoon of the same day are described: deceiving the elders, giving up anger and pride, and playing in the forest.

*Snehaḥ* refers to prema and a small particle of rasa. *Kṛśānuḥ* means fire.

His mother Jaṭilā saw marks on Rādhā. Angry on seeing the marks, taking support of clouds caused by the appearance of the mud of his anger, she torments Rādhā who is like swan.

*Nirabhisandhiḥ* means without *upādhis.* Kum*bhaje dārṇe* means when Agastya rises. Then all the *khañjana* birds rejoice. When it sets, they disappear.

*Prasādhya* means decorating.

I have become angry with the words of Viśākhā. *Abhyarthayan* is in the *ātmanepada* but has a *parasmaipada* meaning. *Dandahyate* means “It burns intensely.”

This is a mixture of *sañcāri-bhavās.*

*Parāṁ koṭim* means most excellent.

*Medhyaḥ* means pure, without fault. Without her, he will actually not wander elsewhere. She casts him away with her hand like a new shoot. He enjoys in any case, but the *mādhavī* without the bee does not look beautiful. Without the *mādhavī,* the bee still enjoys the lotuses with the greatest bliss.

I am a playground of many faults. There are many opportunities to see faults in me. *Arthāntara-nyāsa* is used in the last part of the verse.

*Nirvṛṇoti* means “he attains happiness.”

Vṛndā speaks. The cuckoos (*anya-puṣṭāḥ*) warble with pride in loud voices (*udagrā*). *Niṣpratyūham* means without obstacle. *Śaṣpam* means grass. *Sīmantinyaḥ* means women. May the women perform their household tasks!

From which guru did you take initiation or for what reason did you take initiation.

*Āyatyām* means later according to *Amara-koṣa.*

*Nirmāsyati kim* means “Will she do?”

Kṛṣṇa indicates Rādhā by saying “my friend” but actually the friend is Subala. Subala can take it the meaning in that way only.

*Mat-priyayoḥ* means Rādhā and Lalitā, though actually they are Vṛndā and Subala. *Vyalīkatā* means “not having affection.” They do not seem to have given up indifference.

The beauty of Rādhā tinged with anger or which attracts Kṛṣṇa has manifested. May it not give suffering to the *cakora* Kṛṣṇa.

*Rathāṅga* also means a wheel.

It is understood that she had not spoken to Jatīlā at that time.

*Vikira* means throw.

Confining her to the house he will hide her. *Yadu-rājadhānīm* means Mathurā.

Rādhā, suffering separation from Kṛṣṇa, imparts pain (*bāḍham*) to her friends. She is equal to a sunstone blazing at noon, with cheeks like a white duck (*kāraṇḍava*).

The sound spreading in all directions *(pratimukham*) gives happiness to the ears. Because of this *(tataḥ*) all the *caṭaka* birds (*kalaviṅkavaliḥ*) have assembled, making the sweet soft sound their ear ornaments.

You drink nectar and vomit poison that bewilders the universe. This is not your fault since you have been made with a harsh nature. Another meaning of *dārunā* is wood.

My eyes are happy, having seen my dear friend Subala. He thinks Rādhā is Subala in disguise). Or my eyes are happy having previously seen two friends (Lalitā and Rādhā). *Sakhīnāṁ śikhāmaṇe* (crest jewel of friends) indicates both Subala and Rādhā.

*Dhinoti* means to be pleased. *Āmantrayiṣye* means “I will address.”

Make the tree blossom by giving medicine to make the flowers appear. This means give him bliss by your touch.

*Kādambinī* means a group of clouds. One can recognnize him by proper means. She indicates that is not Subala but Rādhā. Kṛṣṇa can be recognized as by his special quality of dark complexion, with lightning on his neck. Similarly Rādhā can be recognized. The false Rādhā is Subala.

The false Rādhā was Subala. He went to Mukharā’s house.

*Anukaraṇāt* means “because of similarity.” *Hariṇmaṇiḥ* is an emerald.

You, with yellowish attraction (or having attraction for one who is golden or friendly attraction), have no suffering in relation to Rādhā since you are happy in accepting or rejecting her. With bluish attraction (attraction to the dark person), she however suffers concerning you, since she cannot tolerate giving you up.

*Rohiṇī* means red and a constellation called Rohiṇī. *Jyeṣṭhā* means best and is also a constellation. *Citrayā* means being attractive and also is a constellation. *Ārdrāṁ* means giving happiness and an constellation named Ārdrā. You are best (*uttara*) with your two ears (*śravaṇa).* Uttara and Śravaṇa are also constellations. You are joyful with the rising of the stars (*tāra*) or shine with appearance of pearls. *Aśleṣa* is a constllation and also means embrace. *Dākṣiṇyam* means favorable.

*Unnāha* means increase. Cast a glance full of mercy (*kṛpā-lakṣmīvantam* ) at him (*iha).*

For persons desiring (*irmitsataḥ*) prema for Kṛṣṇa *(asmin*) streams of tears do not cease. Because of this (*iti*) the mind should not have attraction for Kṛṣṇa.

*Tapādyāḥ* refers to summer and other seasons. Will the bee not enjoy the wealth of these seasons—the various flowers. But the beauty of spring greatly intoxicates the bee because of the joy of rasa. Though the bee enjoys other seasons, the spring is not indifferent to the bee but gives it bliss. You should be like that.

*Pāṭalā* is the *pārula* plant. *Śata-patrikā* is the rose.

*Kiṁśukaḥ* is the *palāśa* plant Among the gopīs with prema, he criticizes those with uncooperative nature.

This indicates Kṛṣṇa and his friends. They are pleasing externally, but do not have a trace of prema inside.

The meaning is this. By your hands, pots of milk and yogurt containing ghee become devoid of ghee by extraction of the butter. *Sneha* means ghee and affection. What affection do you have? Kṛṣṇa is full of affection but you say he is without affection in reverse of his real quality.

They carry sticks and ropes for killing people or for herding cows. They have minds like stone or are decorated with *manaśīla* mineral. They take shelter of difficult paths or go on paths for herding the cows. Just as thieves take life by using weapons, the boys take life by cutting off prema.

*Kūrcikā is* soured milk also called *khuracinī.* You side with the gopīs out of greed for this.

*Duravacchadaḥ* means attained with difficulty. Like musk the gopīs are hard to attain, intoxicating and slimy. They have three faults. Kṛṣṇa is like the southern wind (*dākṣiṇyataḥ*). The word also means because of being favorable. Thus he is easy to attain. He also is attractive to all people. Kṛṣṇa has these two qualiteis like the spring wind.

*Haṇḍe* is an address to an inferior according to Amara-koṣa. *Śaśādanaḥ* means a hawk. *Prāghuṇa* means a guest.

Along with their wives the flowering creepers the trees offer flowers in the Vṛndāvana forest. Flowering suggests that the creepers are eager without interruption (avyāhatam) on all sides (*abhitaḥ*) to serve bees as the guests. The forest is filled with animals and flocks of playing birds. O Rādhā with beautiful throat! You understand that the bees are fond of the trees and creepers.

When you are present (*te purataḥ*), what is the beauty of the forest for me? What use do I have in describing the beauty of the forest. You are more beautiful.

The Yamunā is without waves, or without lust and anger. It has pure lotuses or is devoid of r*ajas* and *tamas*. It has a host of living beings or has *sattva guṇa*. It shines clearly with blackness. It is honored by Yama, her brother, or by Vaivasvata, or by the rules like non-violence.

*Kokanadam* is a red lotus according to Am*ara-koṣa.*

*Śilīmukhaḥ* means a bee according to *Amara-koṣa. Anurāga-lakṣmīḥ* means a wealth of anurāga but also means the beauty of the red color in the lotus. Who would not be attracted. Even I am attracted.

O Rādhā! Your confusion gives me pleasure. She is described. Your glances moving about because of the buzzing of bees attracted to your ear ornament creates the beauty of a host of bees. Your bracelets jingle from movements of your arms swining in fear.

O slim Rādhā, let the bee drink the auspicious honey.

sa-vyāmohaṁ \| hā dhik hā dhik! kutra gato madhumathanaḥ ity ādibhiḥ \| pūrvokta-madhusūdana-śabdasyārthaṁ kṛṣṇam abhipretya vicchedena khidyantyās tasyā jātaṁ prema-vaicittyaṁ varṇayati \| madhusūdanaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ \|

Did he see unlimited faults in me such as stubbornness? Was he called by some favorite woman (abhīṣṭayā)? dava means forest fire.

andhaṁ karaṇī follows Pāṇini 3.2.56.\|

sakhi vṛnde, rakṣa mām \|

kālī means black. śikhī means fire according to Amara-koṣa kiṁśukais the palāśa tree. ardha-canda is a type of weapon. Candrāvalī also means Candrāvalī, head of a group of gopīs.

Candrāvalī also refers to the head of a group of gopī.

uddhūra-makaranda-mattā ruddhe sāreṇa gandha-visāreṇa \| iha maṅgala-malli-gaṇe rolambā bhramarā hanta guñjanti \| ity ekadaivoccaritena rādhayāpi druta-kāritvaṁ vyañjitam \|

She quickly composes this verse, uttering it once. Kṛṣṇa then repeats it from memory, demonstrating his great ability.

Previously it was described that she threw a stick and shouted.

Having shown three or four days of spring pastimes, the author now presents the autumn pastimes. Only half a day’s pastimes alone are described to impress the heart, while understanding the seriousness of the work in describing these pastimes . The task is hard to accomplish, because of the variety of pastimes such as the rāsa dance etc.

She heard from Padmā.

The question implies fulfillment concerning love. She had come early in the morning with a desire to talk to Lalitā, and see their sorrow on being scolded by Jaṭilā.

She considered as follows. You should place a covering of colored minerals in my hand. What type?

They are minerals taken from a mountain by the king’s servants, or minerals which you should collect from the mountain. The minerals should be from the peak of the mountain (*atuccha-pada-sthitiḥ*), not the lower slopes. This is the normal meaning, to fool Padmā. The real meaning is as follows. *Rāgi-dhātu-paricchada* has eight syllables. Subtract *(mukta*) the syllables *gi* and *ri* (*giriḥ*), where there are also the syllables *tu, ccha, pa* and *da.* The remaining syllables are *rā* and *dhā.* This was Kṛṣṇa’s skill in using the opposing member as a messenger in order to deliver great good fortune for Rādhā. He gave the message by codes.

*Nepathya* means dressing. The meaning is this. Because of Candrāvalī’s great fortune, in one day they must again and again dress her for enjoying with Kṛṣṇa. You however only sometimes dress Rādhā for occasional enjoyment with Kṛṣṇa, because of Rādhā’s lesser fortune.

We laugh because of your inferiority. It is a lie that Candrāvalī always enjoys with Kṛṣṇa. Even if it is true, so what? You know that in front of the sun of Rādhā’s fortune Candrāvalī’s fortune is a dim reflection.

Considering that she is so difficult to attain Kṛṣṇa becomes mad (*samadasya*) like an young elephant hard to control (*gandha-kalabhendra*). He does not enjoy Candrāvalī because she is easy to obtain. He finds it difficult to pray for enjoyment with Rādhā, since she is hard for him to meet. Thus he approaches us. We consider this to be torture (*kadarthana*) for him.

Her intention is to explain this as inferior by showing something superior.

Since you have an honest nature, in revealing the subtle meaning, you should not be shy.

When we are brought under Kṛṣṇa’s control by his sweetness, pacification and gifts, at this time the lac on her feet gradually disappears as proof.

What is the motive for her answer?

This is the actual meaning. Again there is a hint of bringing out further truth in a hidden way.

She is embarrassed on hearing her own glories. She speaks because she sees Padmā’s withered face when she cannot reply properly.

Vṛndāvana is filled with the humming of wandering bees like praisers because of the scent made fickle by the wind from new clusters in the creepers. Or the bees become bound up by the scent. There are millions of excellent *guñja* berries. Vṛndāvana is crowded with peacock feather tails.

The forest has become dark color, like you.

Though the forest is filled *(sandālitā*) with the abundant sweetness of autumn if there are no *khañjana* birds there is no beauty. *Khañjanākṣī* indicates Rādhā.

*He rathāṅgi* means a female c*akravāka* bird and indicates Rādhā. *Rauravīti* means he talks excessively, and cries.

The milk flowers in streams, so that Vṛndāvana becomes filled with rivers of yogurt because of trees flowering unseasonably. Streams of milk arise from the cows and trees flower by hearing the flute. By contact with the sour juice of the flowers, the milk turns to yogurt. *Puṣpya* means blossoming.

*Parimala-tulita* refers to his bull named Padma-gandha. Thus it is said that *padma-gandhaḥ* means fragrant, very dear and a bull.

Out of hatred of Lakṣmī, a co-wife, she cannot say her name. (the friend of Lakṣmī or kamala)

*Sudhākara-maṇḍali* means “O form of the moon.” Decorate the forest by spreading your rays (with your footsteps.)\|

The flute sound is a mystic power described in the Atharva Veda for making women lose their shyness. Atharva-veda has many mantras for cursing. The sound is like chanting mantras for lighting the fire of love. The sound produces fainting in prema, intense love, and loss of shame, bringing about oneness as in *tattvam asi*. The flute has become your enemy. Words like *vaira, śabda, kalah abhra, kaṇva* and *megha* can be made into verbs.

Her mind is beautiful with the best love or her flowers shine with corresponding redness. She has attractive lines drawn on her limbs.

*Medhām* means memory. I will not be able to forget his beauty because of disturbed memory.

*Vilāsa* refers to gestures of indifference etc. *Kandalayasi* means you manifest.

The other meaning is “After taking the life of me, your friend, why do you go far away?”

The other meaning is as follows. Leaving tall Kṛṣṇa, filled with poison, shining with the luster of sapphires, holding a stick and a flute in his belt, you should take the path by the bank of the Yamunā.

What is my fault if I take my cloth which has been taken by her? Asking her, I will take my yellow cloth. Then you will not be able to leave without a top covering. Or wearing my yellow cloth, you cannot go there because of shame.

Among all chaste women or beautiful women I am the topmost or a load to be carried. I am confident because of attachment to or association with Lalitā or my beauty. Therefore, today, do not be a lecher on my path. Or please put me (*mā)* in your arms. Thus the word play is a natural messenger.

She has come for battle (*saṅgaraṁ gamitā*) because of anger. Or she has become enthusiastic for association (*saṅga-raṅgam itā*) because of rasa. She reveals a moonlight smile or she wields a sword.

*Bhairavīt*i means she takes her husband to be Śiva. This is a joke.

She is served by rows of *jhīnti* flowers. The other meaning is as follows. She is served by rows of attractive sakhīs. She spreads wealth for the eyes or spreads beauty to all people.

I am astonished that the peacocks know the proper time.

*Kirīṭaṁ* is the subject.

The other meaning is as follows. Why is the *jhiṇṭi* flower blossoming? Descending from the beautiful mountain of pride, give me your hand for support, since I have gone to the deep forest.

What to speak of giving me bliss, syllables of the lover’s name either heard or seen delight everyone in the world, giving inner satisfaction.

*Vaṣjula* means the *aśoka* tree according to *Amara-koṣa*. According to poets, the *aśoka* tree blossoms when touched by the foot of a woman. Otherwise why would the *aśoka* trees become the object of praise for all the bees who are invited by the fragrance sent from flowers “Please come to my house and drink the honey?” The bees praise, saying, “Who on earth is as generous as you?” *Pātrībhaved* is formed according to the rule *abhūta-tad-bhāve kṛ-bhv-astiyoge sampadyakartari* *cviḥ.* (Pāṇini 5.4.50)

*Vaṅ-mātrā-jita-kāśin* means a conqueror in words only, a boaster. In Vṛndāvana, one Brahmā praises you. This is your pride. Rādhā has been praised by many Brahmās or many born who are golden, for the beauty in each of her limbs. You lifted a mountain with your hand. You are proud of that but you are attracted by the gleam from the edge of her eye many times, not once. You lifted the mountain only once.

*Cañcarīka* means bees. *Tāpiñcha-viñcholī* means groups of *tamāla* trees.

O does, has Kṛṣṇa been seen in this direction? Because of the sound of the flute *(kakalībhir*) mouthfuls of grass fall from your mouths half eaten.

They do not taste the honey (*pauṣpam*) States of confusions, being stunned and turning pale are described in order. *Ibha-vara-gāmī* refers to Kṛṣṇa.

Why do the monkeys embracing *tamāla* branches look at the ground?

*Saṁvidānā* means she knows.

Candrāvalī is reflected inside you.

Enough of joking produced from puns. *Dāna-gandhinā* means “by a person having the fragrance of the *mada* of an elephant.”

The group of foot prints indicates (*ātanoti*) embraces since Kṛṣṇa’s are facing Rādhā’s. They indicate frenzied joking with stubbornness since they prints are in various directions. The order of the pastimes is first irregularly placed prints (joking), then facing each other (embracing), then both facing the same direction (conversing). The order of showing the prints however is different.

There are two meanings. Your hair has come undone or has attained liberation (*muktāḥ*). Your necklace has come unstrung or become free of the three *guṇas.* Your lips are devoid of betel stains or have become liberated in purity. O Rādhā with breasts crushed by his intense embrace! O Rādhā with controlled mind, leading to liberation. The knot in your belt has come undone, or you are free of material bondage. From this I infer that all the disturbance to your hair etc. and your liberation has been done by Kṛṣṇa. Is this proper for chaste women living in Gokula? It is not proper. Actually rather than this, you should have torn his crown and necklace, discolored his lips, scratched his chest and untied his belt. The other meaning is as follows. Is such conduct which gives mere liberation suitable for chaste women of Gokula, who are superior to the best of *tīrthas*? No, it is not, since they are the abodes of *prema-bhakti.*

Among the six seasons, spring, autumn and monsoon especially stimulate passion. Of these, the first is the strongest stimulus, followed by autumn and then monsoon. Pastimes in spring and autumn seasons have been described in brief. Now a pastime at Śrāvaṇa full moon (monsoon) is described with its excellence.

*Bhojendra* means Kaṁsa.

*Karṇejapa* refers to dissension caused by the wicked.

Govardhana means he who has plenty of cows.

*Sarva-maṅgala* refers to Durgā and to Kṛṣṇa.

*Visṛṣṭi* means the whole world. Cupid would be especially useless. But now, Cupid has been successful.

*Madhu-śriyaṁ* means the beauty of spring. Great pastimes will take place by the increase flowers of the monsoon with the presence of spring.

*Prasādhitā* means decorated.

*Vṛtta* means a verse. Padmā took the line p*admāvalambi-karayā, priyayā padmāvataṁsikayā* to mean Kṛṣṇa wants to enjoy with his beloved Candrāvalī, whose hand supports Padmā. *Candraka-maṇḍalena* refers to Kṛṣṇa.

This means it was the Dvādaśī, the festival for wearing *pavitras* (threads tied with knots which are offered to the deity.) The truth is this. On the Dvādaśī they saw the garland in Padmā’s hair. Paurṇamāsī did not know that this was the garland that Rādhā gave Kṛṣṇa. In the morning Rādhā described the skill used in making the garland in order to reveal the reason for Padmā’s pride. Hearing about the garland, Lalitā began to lament. Thus she said “Now I understand.” The next day she decided to go there.

*Sthāne* means suitable according to *Amara-koṣa*. It was very wrong for Kṛṣṇa to do this.

His friends had understood that the wind had taken the garland.

*Pūrveṇa gaurī-tīrtham* means a place to the east but near Gaurī-tīrtha. This use of the word *pūṛveṇa* is explained in *enav anyatarasyām adūre 'pañcamyāḥ* (Pāṇini 5,3.35)

*Kuḍuṅgasy*a means “of Kṛṣṇa.”

*Kumbhī* means elephant.

You have become a vessel of honey of your beauty. *Bhṛṅgāra* means a vessel according to *Amara-koṣa.*

You are not cooperative, but your assistants are.

Since she possesses a host of lotuses, she does not have faults at any time. Or the moon, nor rising at night, shines at all times. She is favorable to me, or the moon is full, though in the dark phase of the moon.

*Udarkaṁ* means at a later time.

The other meaning is “This pond, filled with many women, has waves of *mādhurya-rasa* for Kṛṣṇa.”

The other meaning is as follows. Endowed with great *anurāga* for me, surrounded by friends, shining with attractive ear ornaments, you, like the lotus, spread beauty to all beauty with the appearance of *śṛṅgāra-rasa*.

Why should Kṛṣṇa make our sakhī wither? Wither refers to having less fortune that Rādhā.

The other meaning is “Candrāvalī has attraction for the hero Govardhana Malla”. *Sura* and *śura* sometimes exchange their spelling. She spreads her bliss (*āmoda*). O moon *(tārādhīśa*)! Or O lord of Rādhā! Do not put your hand on Candrāvalī since you only have temporary attraction for her.

Kṛṣṇa has not committed offense. The lotus, given up by Lakṣmī, fades. It is well known that Lakṣmī does not stay among the lotuses at night. Or another meaning is “At night, why does Candrāvalī not meet with Padmā?”

The groups of creepers are gopī friends. The *svarṇa-yūthī* is Candrāvalī. The tears of Candrāvalī indicate her strong prema.

*Idindira-vṛndāni* means a group of bees. The meaning is “I, the shelter of all others, shine near you.”

*Gaurava-bhājām* means that she has *ghṛta-sneha,* which affection is filled with respect.

Anu*rāga-bhaṭa* refers to *madhu-sneha* of Rādhā. *Bhaṭa* is a warrior.

The deer is Kṛṣṇa. Lalitā indicates that Candrāvalī should give up Kṛṣṇa, but Candrāvalī thinks that is impossible for Kṛṣṇa to do this. Thus she smiles.

The other meaning is “You are always desired by Kṛṣṇa, O lover of Kṛṣṇa.”

To inform Padmā that his previous words about the deer desiring the doe were only stated to attract her. There is another meaning. Actually, I have attraction to all the gopīs related to Rādhā, what to speak of having attraction for Rādhā. *Mada*, as the ultimate manifestation of *madhu-sneha*, gives Kṛṣṇa the greatest happiness. Let me take an oath that I have no attraction for those who are unfavorable to her.

All the bees thirstily kiss the cheek of the king of elephant, though constantly disrespected by the elephant flapping his ears. The thirsty elephant goes to the pond. The pond does not go to the elephant. Just as some gopīs desiring enjoyment meet with Kṛṣṇa, though they are not respected and do not give him a particle of pleasure, he meets with Rādhā and attains the highest bliss.

She smiles in order to indicate that she will make a verse to neutralize the question and answer of the two. The shield of the husband of Lakṣmī is called “a hundred moons,” since it has pictures of a group of moon. *Citra-phala* means a slab for painting. Mādhavī in the riddle can mean the husband of Lakṣmī (*mā*). Candrāvalī can mean “many moons.”

*Avadāta-śīlā* means pure in character. *Avadāta* means pure according to *Amara-koṣa.*

Those two described the excellence of their group by a riddle, but we two have made the meaning different. How can we make a riddle that they cannot change? She then speaks. O solver of attractive riddles! Dear friend, by what name is Mādhava known in the world?

*Abhikhyā* means “name” according to *Amara-koṣa.* He is known by the name Rādhā. This is stated in the Vedas. Rādhayā mādhavo devo mādhavenaiva rādhikā vibhrajate: Mādhava shines with Rādhā and Rādhā shines with Mādhava. Thus they are popularly called Rādhā-mādhava. The compound using apposition indicates her supreme position. Deities such as Bindu-mādhava and Veṇī-mādhava are also named in this way. This is a pretext for pointing out Kṛṣṇa.

Vaiśākha month is called Mādhava and Rādha month according to the dictionary. In the phrase *mādhava-rādhau* the word *mādhava* is irregularly placed first out of respect, since he is well known. Kṛṣṇa has given another explanation in order to show their ability to give explanations.

The other meaning is “Be happy with the rasas of lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa.”

The bee is compared to Kṛṣṇa, since it is greedy to taste the nectar. The bee is absorbed in the water lily which blossoms in the evening, or Kṛṣṇa is absorbed in Rādhā--she who make the lotus insignificant (*kumudvatī*), and who is joyful in spite of the lover’s many faults (*pradoṣe*) as long as the water lily does not see the group of lotuses, or as long as Rādhā does not see Candrāvalī, whose friend is Padmā *(padmālī*).

Viśākhā constellation (Rādhā) is seen shining with the stars as long as the moon does not shine in the sky which is as dark as a *tamāla* tree. Rādhā is seen shining along with Viśākhā and other friends as long as Candrāvalī does not remain with Kṛṣṇa.

She laughs because in one verse she negates the meaning of Padmā’s and Śaibyā’s verses. The other meaning is “With the appearance of the excellent beauty of Rādhā, Candrāvalī loses her beauty.”

*Surabheḥ* means “of spring.”

Editor’s note: Though it is not spring but monsoon, the season has become like spring by Vṛndā’s arrangement.

What creeper does not blossom when Kṛṣṇa appears? I offer respects to Rādhā, named as the consort of Mādhava.

*Ammo* is a local expression to indicate anger.

The meaning is this. When your bad conduct becomes known, out of fear of royal punishment, your father will be brought before the king by his troops, since you will disappear in the forest. He will be perplexed in shame and sorrow, saying “What a lawless son I have produced.”

Seeing Candrāvalī without a veil, I become agitated.

What is the fault in the western direction which is loyal to the lord of its life, Varuṇa (presiding deity of the west)? What is the fault of Candrāvalī in following her husband Malla? But lord of its life also means Kṛṣṇa. What is the fault of the sun which removes night or faults (*doṣa*)? Or what is the fault in Kṛṣṇa who steals women in the night? By this pun Lalitā discards fault in the sun and Kṛṣṇa. The twilight, making the western horizon and the sun red, produces the evening. Padmā, making Candrāvalī and Kṛṣṇa meet after producing attraction between them, is at fault. Padmā should be scolded.

Beautiful glory of spring plays in Gaurī-tīrtha. Or Rādhā, your beautiful form of brilliance or wealth, plays at Gaurī-tīrtha. *Gandha-phalī-dvandvaṁ* means two *campaka* flowers.

*Kamalām* means Lakṣmī.

Kṛṣṇa becomes supremely beautiful because of the affectionate care of Yaśodā. He is the culmination of her good fortune.

Tāṇḍavika is the name (*hūtir*) of the peacock *(aṇḍajaḥ) Madiraḥ* is the restless *khañjana* bird. The peacock cannot live for a moment without seeing Kṛṣṇa.

*Puṇḍraka-maṇḍape* means “in the bower of *mādhavī* creepers.”

She again looked with joy. Kṛṣṇa, approaching Rādhā from behind, covered her eyes with his hands. Rādhā’s path was indicated by her many footprints which indicated her path, clearly marked everywhere on the ground.

Her frown should her disapproval. But the frown was endowed with a smile, which indicated the highest approval. She forbade him by saying “No, no” but was overcome with intoxication. This is a *sattvika-bhava,* choking of the voice. This indicates her permission. She blocked him with her hands to indicate she did not desire his touch but with boldness dissipated *(viśrāntoddhati*), since the bliss on experiencing the touch of his hand made her boldness impossible. This again expresses her extreme desire for him. She cried to express sorrow but it was dry crying.

*Nairañjanyaṁ* can mean being without cosmetics or being merged in Brahman. Your breasts have given up the color of *kuṁkuma (rāgitam)* or have lost all attraction to material life. Desire for union with Kṛṣṇa or desire for yoga makes your chest tremble. The skirt has a desire to undo the knot or has a desire for liberation (*mumukṣām*).

*Medhyāma* means “without the contamination of lust.” Tasting (*dhayan*) what is purer, who could be completely satisfied? *Premaiva gopa-rāmāṇāṁ kāma ity agamat prathām*: it is well known that the *kāma-rūpa-bhakti* of the gopīs is prema. (BRS 1.2.285)

The bower is attractive pearls and center jewel of the necklace scattered about. *Piśunayati* means indicate.

*Ghusṛṇam* is *kuṁkumam.*

She played in the dust. Her hair was bound with a colored thread. Her hair was only a *gokarṇa* in length. A *gokarṇa* is the distance from the thumb to the ring finger. Her ears were newly pierced. From which guru did she learn her skill? *Bata* indicates surprise. Even now she is only a little girl.

*Kādambīm* means “made of *kadamba* flowers.”

His body has nail marks shining brightly like thin threads dyed by *mañjiṣṭha.*

*Bhramarakair* means locks of hair on the forehead.

O woman with beautiful hips, it is proper that you, desired by me, are served by the *atimukta* flowers. I am the object of meditation for many liberated persons. *Atimukta* refers to those completely liberated as well as the *mādhavī* flower, according to *Amara-koṣa.*

Today all the cows head for Gokula, though the third *yāma* has not ended, while the beauty of the monsoon season is manifested everywhere. \|

Her two brows conquer the beauty of the bow of Cupid, who fights fiercely.

Kakkhaṭī speaks these words, which are supposedly spoken by Candrāvalī’s followers.

He teaches the flute to kill the world or makes the world full of love (*mara*). He does this without thinking of the sin. If our ears are bitten by the sound of the flute, we will fall under its control. A monkey which dances according to the will of its master is called a play deer (*krīḍā-kuraṅga*).

Spite has attained the place in your heart and thus beneficial advice cannot enter.

The other meaning is “Who does not know my (*sva*) essence *(sāram*), Kṛṣṇa?”

She wants to bring him to see his wife Rādhā associating with Kṛṣṇa.

The creator made your feet into lotuses and your thighs into young banana trunks. Because tender objects cannot be permanent without a stiff support, he made your heart into a thunderbolt.

O woman dear to women of Gokula or O Kṛṣṇa dear to the women of Gokula. *Preyasī* indicate masculine as well as feminine. For instance *bahu-preyasī* means a king dear to many. It is said that the comparative form with *iyas* should not be used in a *bahuvrīhi* compound.However a short vowel is forbidden. A long vowel, similar to the feminine form is permitted. *Pravaṇāṁ* means respectful.

This is an answer to the request “May Kṛṣṇa’s mercy be granted.”

There is no doubt about this.

O traditional *devatā* of the cowherd women! or O devatā of all the cowherd women! Be merciful to him so that I am not separated from my master (Abhimanyu or Kṛṣṇa).

I have been controlled by the ropes of your new devotion, difficult to perform for persons who have controlled their senses. You are the crest jewel of chaste women. Therefore stay in Gokula. Do not go to Mathurā. Following Jaṭilā’s order, always go to the forest to worship me.

The chaste woman who is accused by others destroys their lives. Durgā is the supreme deity. This means that if one does not have faith in her words, one will die.

*Yaśodā mātā* means “he whose mother is Yaśodā.” *Hanta* expresses joy.

*Sādhīyān* means more or most excellent.

Saṁvatsara 1589 = 1533 AD.
